# Changelog

All notable changes to the **workflows-core** plugin are recorded here.
Format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).
Versions follow semver at the plugin level.
A section headed `— Unreleased` has not been published yet; where more than one of them stands, they all ship together in the next release.

## [1.67.0] — 2026-10-28

Take with `security-workflows` 0.7.0, whose `/scan` hands this release's `specs-preflight` and `commit-artifacts` the `register_rebuild` input that lets the replay below rebuild a security register both sides changed — to-fix LC11 (live checks of 2026-10-26, run 6); and with `dev-workflows` 4.49.0 and `product-workflows` 3.59.0, whose session-cost and session-feedback pages cite §3.1's list of the setters of `specs_git: misrooted`, which only this release completes, and whose resume pages gloss the flag with `session-hygiene` §6's *Stop* too — to-fix D27.

### Added
- **A run settles its own rejected session-file push** (`specs-repo-git` §4 step 6). Where `origin` rejects a push of the default branch or a plugin branch as non-fast-forward outside session-branch mode — at the end of a run, or in §3.4's flush and its retry of other plugin branches — the run no longer prints `pull --rebase --autostash` for you to run. It calls the new `session-branch.py replay`, which fetches `origin`'s branch, measures `push-scope` again against it, and replays each session-file commit of the plugin's that the push would publish onto it with `git merge-tree --write-tree --merge-base` and `commit-tree`, touching none of the checkout, the index or any ref; drops a commit whose change `origin` already holds; then moves the branch in one locked transaction — `update-ref --stdin` prepared, `read-tree -m -u`, committed or aborted — which refuses, with nothing moved, where it would write over a path you changed, left untracked or ignore, or where another session switches the checkout to or from the branch while it moves — where the checkout stands is read at the move itself, never trusted from before the replay, so a branch the checkout left during the replay moves by `update-ref` alone, and once more under the move's lock; and only then pushes the replayed tip, at most three attempts where another writer pushes meanwhile. An appended session file both sides changed keeps both sides' entries, an overwritten one (`resume.md`, `pr-draft.md`, `*-implementation-gaps.md`) takes the run's version whole, refused where `origin` deleted it, a security register both sides changed is rebuilt from the merged run records by the `register.py` the caller hands over, and any other path both sides changed refuses the replay. It refuses too where this git's `merge-tree` lacks `--write-tree` or `--merge-base` (git 2.40 or later has both), a rebase, merge, cherry-pick or revert is in progress, a lock stands, the fetch fails, or another worktree has the branch checked out. Where step 5's push carried `-u`, the replay sets the upstream itself, since its own push, from a commit id, sets none (§1 rule 8). The Bash call carries a 420000 ms timeout over the script's own bounded fetches and pushes, and the script holds SIGTERM, SIGHUP and SIGINT while it moves a branch; a call the harness ends at that timeout reports the push rejected, saying the branch may already stand on the replay and which run retries it.
- **§6's rows for it**: *replayed and pushed*, *already there* and *moved, not pushed*, each naming every finding a register rebuild renumbered (`[SEC#<from>] → [SEC#<to>] (<origin>, the local number | origin's number)`), and their `Specs preflight:` forms inside §3.4's flush and retry. The *push rejected* row now says nothing moved, names why in one clause from a closed list, and advises `git pull --rebase --no-autostash` — save where another session switched the checkout during the move, where it names none — which refuses up front, touching nothing, while anything is uncommitted. The old remedy's `--autostash` gave back a staged-then-edited file unstaged and, where putting your changes back conflicted, left conflict markers in your own file and a stash behind while still exiting 0; `--no-autostash` is spelled out because a `rebase.autoStash` setting does the same to a bare `--rebase`. In session-branch mode, a plugin branch's *push rejected* remedy is wrapped in the session branch's `lift` and `put-back`, since the overlay keeps session files modified, which the switch and the pull refuse.
- **`specs-preflight` and `commit-artifacts` take one optional input, `register_rebuild: <absolute path>`** (§7): the `register.py` a security register is rebuilt with, which a `workflows-core` reference cannot name for itself. A caller that passes nothing gets the replay all the same, save that a register both sides changed refuses it.
- §1 rule 4 names the replay as its exception beside §8's checkout-free merges — one that, unlike them, does move the checkout — and §8's opening names the replay as the script's too. `phase-handoff` §1 rule 4 does not take that exception: a deliverable push rejected as non-fast-forward is still reported, never replayed.

### Changed
- **Session-branch mode merges block-shaped session files by entry set; git's `union` driver merges `follow-ups.md` alone** (§8.2). `union` matches lines, not entries, so two distinct entries sharing lines — two same-day `/implement` blocks on one unit, or two feedback entries of one date, command and category — became one, with git exiting 0. `implementation.md`, `_security.md` and the feedback and cost files now settle as one side's version followed by each entry of the other's that it lacks, refused where the headers differ, an entry of the merge base was edited or removed on one side alone, or the line endings differ; `follow-ups.md`, one task per line, keeps `union` and is refused in mixed line endings. A merge of the default branch or of `origin`'s copy of the session branch that the rule refuses is not made at all, its paths listed in `sync.conflict`, and in `sync.conflicts` with their source; a working copy the rule cannot settle with the branch's version stays out of the commit, as it is in your working tree, under a new `unsettled` output and its own §6 append. The marked `info/attributes` block names no block-shaped file any more, and is rewritten on each clone's next run, so a merge you make by hand stops on a conflict rather than fusing two entries. An entry only your session branch holds is put back where a working copy dropped it, so until the session pull request merges no hand edit removes one.
- **`security-register-format` §1 and §2** say the replay rebuilds the register itself and names each renumbered finding, that its rebuild writes no record, so the next run that records reports the move again with the lines to correct, and that the check under each move also runs after each replay that brings `origin`'s records in. The settle steps — `register.py rebuild`, `git add`, `git rebase --continue` — apply only where the replay refused or could not run.
- The references page describes the replay, and the environment page what the entry-set merge means for an entry you edit or remove by hand in session-branch mode, and for a file a run leaves out of its commit as `unsettled`.

### Fixed
- **Every setter of `specs_git: misrooted` is named** (to-fix D27). `specs-repo-git` §3.1 named its notices and `specs-root-check`'s stop, but not `session-hygiene` §6's *Stop*, which sets the flag where a `/design` or `/specify` run finds, at any of §6's three points, its environment no longer the one its session file recorded, or, at the last two, that record unreadable or missing. §3.1, §4 step 1, §7's caller contract, `phase-handoff` §2.1, `cost-emission` §8, `feedback-emission`'s ladder and `session-hygiene` §1 now name it, and §4 step 1 and `phase-handoff` §2.1 re-emit its stop line, `RESUME_ENVIRONMENT_CHANGED:`, where they re-emit the notice that set the flag. §6's *Misrooted* row read `Specs repo: NOT COMMITTED — SPECS_PATH is misplaced`, false after a *Stop* on an `origin`, `$REPOS_PATH` or loader mismatch; it now reads `… SPECS_PATH is misplaced, or the run's environment no longer matches the one it recorded; see the notice below`.
- **A refused, failed or rejected push is retried by a later run, not always the next** (`specs-repo-git` §4 step 6, §6, §8.4). The *push failed* and unrecorded *push refused* lines, the session branch's *push rejected* line and the sentences behind them said the next run retries or pushes; a run whose preflight stops at a guard (§3.3) retries only a branch it commits on, and a blocked or misrooted run none, so they now say a later run.

## [1.66.0] — 2026-10-28

Take with `product-workflows` 3.58.0, whose `/brd-interview` runs the gate this release adds to §3.4 and names in §3.7, and with `dev-workflows` 4.48.2, whose `/ready` and pages name it — batch B25 (FBB-B44).

### Added
- **`phase-handoff` §3.4 gains a `/brd-interview` row on the parent's `brd/brd-defect-log.md`**, which falls back on `absent`. `/brd-interview`'s Phase 0 step 8a runs `require-on-main` on that log only where it carries an `open` entry of class `unsourced` on a row the slice's ledger records `covered-here` or `deferred-to`, before step 9 reads anything, because the command may now offer to write `resolved-by: <SLICE-KEY>/[CG#n]` there on the operator's confirmation. On `absent`, as on every stopping row, it makes no offer, writes nothing into the log and prints the value to write by hand. §3.4's closing paragraph says `/brd-interview` is among the consumers that stop on `absent` for its `grounding/code-grounding.md` row alone.

### Changed
- **`phase-handoff` §3.7 names three callers that read a stopping row without stopping**, `/brd-interview`'s step 8a beside `/ready` and `/brd-reconcile`'s three sites. At that gate a stopping row withholds only the offer to close a defect, and row C's *Cancel* is reported as the operator's decline, never as an unsettled log.
- **§4.0's `brd/brd-defect-log.md` row names both gates on it and both writers of resolutions**, `/brd-reconcile` and `/brd-interview`, and §4.1's spanning-set example names `/brd-interview`'s handoff beside `/brd-reconcile`'s. The `require-on-main` entry file is regenerated.

## [1.65.1] — 2026-10-28

Take with `product-workflows` 3.57.0, whose `/epics` hands off the files this release classifies, names the next command per Epic as §2.7 now says and hands `doc-fixer` a file path — batch B24 (PRD-B6, FBB-B20, FBA-G2, FBA-G4).

### Changed
- **`phase-handoff` §4.0 classifies a PRD folder's `CLAUDE.md` as advisory and a PRD folder's Epic index page as unread** — what `/epics`' maintenance agents now edit before its handoff and declare in it; the harness loads the first, and no command opens the second. No array moves: the set takes its gated `epic.md`'s class. §4.3's falling-back bullet no longer says `/epics`' set holds `_coverage.md` beside its Epics and nothing else, and names its run as one whose Epics are all new or unchanged.
- **`doc-fixer` takes its reviewer or style-checker output as the absolute path of a file holding it**, as well as pasted, reading the file whole first and refusing where it cannot be read. `/epics` passes a path; `/document` still pastes.
- **`components`' consumer note names `/epics` Phase 5's use of `component-of`**, placing each scanned cross-component call's caller for its contract-omission check.
- **`phase-handoff` §2.7's next-command sentence is true of an edit** (FBB-B20). The body said the next command *will not run* until the pull request merges, false where the artifact is an edit of one the default branch already carries: the next command runs there, on the default branch's copy, or stops on the difference. It now says the next command will not run on what the pull request carries until it merges — a new artifact stops it at rows D and E, the branch's owner included, while on an edited one it meets the default branch's copy, save a consumer sharing the producer's prefix and key, which row B lets read the branch's copy — and that a producer whose set may hold either says which per artifact by the test that picks its consent array, as `/epics` does.

## [1.65.0] — 2026-10-27

Take with `dev-workflows` 4.48.0 and `security-workflows` 0.6.0, whose `/implement`, `/conformance` and `/scan` say the merge rests on the subject alone in the new left-behind case, and whose pages describe the merged test below; each runs §5 from the loaded reference, so an older one still runs. Closes to-fix C10.

### Fixed
- **`implementation-format` §5's corroborated subject leg walks the branch for the merged pull request's head** (C10). `<floor>` is the commit the earliest earlier block on the same branch records, where it is an ancestor of the tip and not of `<ref>` — never an earlier pull request's merged commit under a branch name cut again — or else the block's recorded commit, where it is an ancestor of the tip. The leg tries the tip's first-parent commits down to `<floor>`, at most twenty and never into `<ref>`'s history (`^<fork>`), then `<floor>`, then the recorded commit. Where neither commit is an ancestor of the tip, as after a rebase, it tries the tip and the recorded commit alone. Each candidate is tested against `S` and then `<ref>`, and the leg passes on the first that adds nothing. The walk never goes below the block's own work, so an empty first commit, a stacked branch's base or a cherry-picked first commit cannot stand for the pull request.
- **A conflict no longer passes the leg.** An open pull request whose work conflicts with another `[<key>]` commit — a teammate's, or an earlier run's from a branch this run did not continue — now reads as open, not as `after_merge`. One conflict still passes on the subject alone, the **left-behind case**: the tip is the local branch, `origin/<branch>` does not resolve, and the block records `pushed: true`. That case is stated by what the clone holds, not by which run removed the ref, and each consumer says so in a line of its own — the tip conflicts and `origin/<branch>` is gone — not that the tip was not compared. A call that compared nothing, or an empty walk, still passes on the subject alone.
- **The timing case is fixed where it can be.** A later run's commit on the same branch, committed before the squash but pushed after it, now reads `after_merge` true, since the earlier run's commit is the squash's head. Where that commit was made after the squash, `--since` excludes `S`, and the leg finds no commit to corroborate, as before.
- **Remaining misses.** Past twenty commits the leg errs toward open. A teammate's cherry-pick of an earlier same-branch run's commit is the remaining miss toward merged.
- §5's misses, `after_merge`, step 2's consumer note and the rules summary are rewritten to match.
- `implementation-format` §4 no longer calls the widened key boundary a change still to make: CORE-G2 was measured and closed as not taken, and the paragraph gives the reasons (no known set to resolve a branch name against, the boundary every reader shares, and a squash normally carrying its keys).

## [1.64.0] — 2026-10-27

Take with `dev-workflows` 4.47.0 and `product-workflows` 3.53.0, whose `/design` and `/specify` cite this release's `session-hygiene` §6 — to-fix PR8 (live checks of 2026-10-26).

### Added

- **`session-hygiene` §6, the resume-environment check, in a part of its own (`resume-environment`).** A command that keeps a session file it resumes from records `$SPECS_PATH`'s real path, the specs repository's `origin`, `$REPOS_PATH` and the reference loader's install as the file's `## Run environment` section, and compares them on a resume, before the deliverable write that leads to the handoff, and before the first write of its emitter tail. On a mismatch it asks — on a resume, go on in the current environment (recommended unless `origin` differs) or stop; later, stop or go on pinned to the recorded environment where it still exists — and never goes on silently. *Stop* sets `specs_git: misrooted`, reusing that flag's handling, since the run's specs root is not the one it recorded. §1–§5 stay guidance-only; §6 is outside their contract.

## [1.63.0] — 2026-10-27

Take with `product-workflows` 3.52.0, whose `/prd-ground` Phase 3 asks the question this release's `grounding-format` §4 cites, and whose `--repos-path` flag and `/epics` re-review offer this release's `escalation-rules` names — to-fix D7, D8, D9 (batches B24, B26).

### Changed
- **`grounding-format` §4 step 1: a clone with no readable `origin` keeps the name an earlier `baselines.md` entry records it by** where the operator confirms, at `/prd-ground` Phase 3, that it is the same repository — a renamed directory, or an entry a run before `product-workflows` 3.33.3 wrote. It said the basename alone, so a renamed clone was recorded under two names.
- `escalation-rules`' *Required path environment variable unset* says `/prd-ground` tests its `--repos-path` value, where one is given, in place of `${REPOS_PATH:-/workspace}` (product-workflows 3.52.0, D7).
- `escalation-rules`' *Edits after a verdict that is not BLOCK — the unspent re-review* names `/epics`' split at its last count of an Epic an *Epic size* finding named among the ways it edits the reviewed artifact (product-workflows 3.52.0, D8).

## [1.62.0] — 2026-10-27

Take with `dev-workflows` 4.44.0 and `product-workflows` 3.51.0, whose `/design` and `/specify` dispatch this release's `overlap-reader` — to-fix PR5 (live checks of 2026-10-26, run 2).

### Added
- **`overlap-reader`, a read-only agent pinned to Opus, runs `/design`'s whole-design and `/specify`'s whole-specification overlap read** (PR5). Dispatched before the play-back that precedes the first review, with the draft (none where `/design` writes it after its grill), the session file's decisions, for `/design` the specification the design implements — whose stories, criteria, test cases and scope lines are members too, read as what a decision must keep — the applicable ARD files and the inherited `[AD#N]` Rules, and, for `/design`'s second read after a write-once grill, each change or addition the writing made, it loads `grilling-technique` and lists each of *What a requirement overlaps*' five kinds, one entry per member, the kinds its caller names first listed first, and returns the lists, the counts the play-back's `Overlap read:` line needs and each conflict: a pair that gives one situation two outcomes, or a requirement, decision or scope line that departs from an `[AD#N]` Rule. Its Rule list reads each Rule's quantifiers — *every*, *once*, *within N seconds*, *always*, *never* — against every member touching the behaviour the Rule binds: a figure added on the same path counts toward the Rule's bound, and a sibling Rule's recovery of a step that was lost or failed is no leave to skip the step. A departure already recorded as an ARD deviation — by a record of that departure, not only of its Rule, in the draft or as a decision of the session file — is returned with where it is recorded, so its caller does not ask it again; a record of another departure from the same Rule does not count. It writes, asks and settles nothing; its callers put each conflict to the user. Run 2 of the live checks, `/specify BOOK-1-02`, fired both of PR5's triggers: the whole-specification play-back never reached the screen, and both reads printed `4 [AD#N] Rules — no conflict` beside three departures — a skipped notice and a racing-sale exclusion from [AD#4], read as kept because [AD#5]'s sweep would recover them, and a 5-second send budget past [AD#9]'s 30 seconds — which the reviewer found, the second after the cap was spent. The blind replay had shown agents in a fresh context reading the same rule correctly, so the read moves into one. `grilling-technique`'s overlap rule gains no words.
- The agents page lists it, and the README, the docs index, the getting-started and the workflow pages count eleven agents.

## [1.61.1] — 2026-10-27

Take with `dev-workflows` 4.43.0, whose `/conformance` walk this release's picker row describes (batch B05).

### Fixed
- `escalation-rules`' closed-vocabulary list names the `/conformance` decision picker's cluster form: one of the three decisions for every item of a cluster, *Split* into a question per item, or a typed `Cancel`, which ends the run — never a fourth decision; and *No operator is trapped* says the cluster form is the one form of that picker whose `Cancel` is the free-text answer the walk's preamble names, its four slots being full (FBB-B26).

## [1.61.0] — 2026-10-27

Take with `dev-workflows` 4.42.0 and `product-workflows` 3.50.0, whose `/design` sends a change or addition made while writing to this release's whole-artifact gate, whose `/design` and `/specify` hold that gate for the play-back their overlap reads precede, and whose `/epics` presents this release's `/epics` form of *No repos derivable* and holds out of `doc-fixer` the `MAJOR` findings this release's review rules say it no longer takes — to-fix LC4, LC7, LC8 (live checks of 2026-10-26).

### Changed
- **`grilling-technique` *The confirmation gate*: the whole-artifact gate follows what was written, not how it was written** (LC4). It fires once more over the whole artifact, before the first reviewer dispatch, handoff or commit that follows the interview, whenever the writing changed or added to anything a gate confirmed — a decision, a test, a criterion, a number, changed or added while its part was being written or by a part written after its gate — however many gates the run held, and whether the run wrote its artifact section by section or once after the grill; its play-back names each such change or addition and plays back only those. It fired only "where a caller wrote its artifact section by section", so a run that played the whole artifact back once and wrote it afterwards had no gate for a change made while writing. In all seven `/design` transcripts that wrote `design.md`, every grill question came before the first write, and three of them played the whole design back once and wrote it afterwards; run 3 of the live checks added a test to a confirmed decision while writing, said so in narration only and dispatched the reviewer. `/create-prd`'s one measured run wrote the same way: eight questions, then one write. **The change also narrows the gate**: a section-by-section run whose writing changed or added nothing no longer gets it — a caller that hangs something on that play-back, as `/design` and `/specify` hang their overlap reads, holds it in its own text. The bullet also names a write-once run's gate before its write, says a change told only in narration has passed no gate, and no longer calls the gate "not a single end-of-interview event": a run that writes section by section, as its caller's paragraph says it does or lets it, closes each section's gate as the interview settles it or after the last question, and a run that writes once after the grill closes one over the whole artifact. *At the gate, play the understanding back* says the whole-artifact play-back names each change or addition with what the gate had confirmed.
- The references page describes the gate.
- **`escalation-rules`' *No repos derivable — /epics* gains an `/epics` form** (LC7): `["Choose repos to scan", "Proceed without code scan", "Cancel"]`, whose first option is followed by `/epics` Phase 1's repository picker — the session's own repository first, recommended, where one stands, then the clones under `$REPOS_PATH`, capped per `epic-picker` *The cap*, beside *Turn code scan off* — not by a plain-text question, so its label carries no `(you'll be prompted)`. In run 4 of the live checks the plain-text question was easy to overlook from inside the repository the run should have scanned. `/idea`, `/specify` and `/prd-ground` keep their forms.
- `escalation-rules`' *Findings a review leaves open — the offer to apply them* and *A BLOCK review's other findings — folded into its fix cycle* say that `/epics`' `doc-fixer` takes the `MAJOR` findings whose fix is only an edit; they said it took every `MAJOR`, which `/epics` 3.50.0 no longer hands it where the fix needs a decision (LC8).

## [1.60.0] — 2026-10-27

Take with `dev-workflows` 4.41.0, `product-workflows` 3.49.0 and `docs-workflows` 1.26.0, whose review steps, handoffs, pickers and offers cite or restate this release's rules, and with `security-workflows` 0.5.0, whose `register.py` prints the `reserved_moves` key and `rebuild`'s `numbers` this release's `security-register-format` states — to-fix LC1, LC2, LC3, LC6, LC9, LC10, PR7 (live checks of 2026-10-26).

### Added
- `escalation-rules` *A BLOCK review's other findings — folded into its fix cycle* (LC3). Where a command that fixes inline — `/create-prd`, `/update-prd`, `/create-ard`, `/specify`, `/design`, `/prd-proposal`, `/brd-proposal` — has a review return `BLOCK` and go to its fix cycle while it also leaves open a `MAJOR`, `MINOR` or `NIT` finding whose fix is only an edit (in `/create-prd`, `/update-prd` and `/create-ard`, which settle decisions as they apply, also one that needs a decision; where the command triages, a survivor), the run asks once, before the fix cycle, `["Fold them into this fix cycle (Recommended)", "Fix the BLOCKERs only — the rest wait for the re-review"]`, the question's own text listing every such finding, each needing a decision marked so. A finding folded in is applied in the fix cycle and read against what it overlaps, so the one re-review covers it; on *Fold*, those three commands grill each decision in the fix cycle, and every other command leaves it open. On *Fix the BLOCKERs only* the findings wait for the re-review's offer where it makes one, as before; one that needs a decision makes no offer on its own, and is recorded where *A finding left open that needs a decision is recorded in the artifact* names the command, and otherwise listed in the final report and the pull request. It is no offer and raises no cap. Four live runs on the e2e test machine asked this question in four wordings of their own: `/specify` session `2a06e283` (2026-10-26) and `/design` sessions `0c71a857` (2026-10-26), `45744f83` (2026-10-24) and `3a0d7096` (2026-10-23). `/epics`, `/document` and `/implement`, whose `BLOCK` path dispatches a fixer, are outside it, and the rule names where their other findings go.
- **`security-register-format` §2: a reserved record's moves are reported apart.** `register.py plan --reserve-dir` numbers the checkout's own findings as the checkout holds them — its `origins`, every row's number and its `renumbered` — so a person's answers keep their numbers, and lists each move the reserved records' merge will make to one of them as `reserved_moves`, `{origin, from, to, run, held_by}`, numbering that merge with the record this run's `commit` writes — the checkout's moves no record holds yet, sorting last, so a finding that walked keeps its number unless a reserved record published it first — `run` being the reserved record the move waits on: the one that takes `from` from another finding, else the earliest-sorting one that gives the moved finding its new number — moving one of its allocations off `from`, or else publishing `to` for it — else the earliest-sorting one that publishes any number for it, else `null`; and `held_by` the reserved record whose `renumbered` already holds that very move, else `null`. Each is provisional: `to` holds only once every reserved record has merged, and their branches merge at different times, so no follow-up line that cites `from` is corrected on the move's strength. After each pull, `register.py rebuild` — whose output now carries `numbers`, every allocated origin's `[SEC#n]`, another name for a finding included — tells the cases apart: a move from `from` to the finding's number among the moves no record holds is reported, with the lines, by the first run that records; a finding off `from` with no such move is held by a record, and its lines are corrected by hand. The check stops once the `run` record and the `held_by` record, where it names one, are both in the checkout and the finding stands under `to` or a run that records has run since both arrived — never on `to` alone, which a held move reaches before its holder arrives; where `run` is `null`, and so `held_by`, once the finding stands under `to` or a run that records has run since this one (to-fix LC10, live checks of 2026-10-26).

### Changed
- `escalation-rules` *Findings a review leaves open — the offer to apply them* (LC6): the question's own text lists the findings, one line per finding (its severity, where it is and what the edit would change), and never points at a list printed elsewhere. Two live offers made after a re-review, in `/specify` and `/epics`, asked about findings no text had shown, one of them as "listed above". §0's overflow consequence now says the run states a list longer than the prompt where the dialog shows it, as `next-phase-offer`'s *overflow rule* step 3 says.
- `escalation-rules` *Manual fix notes — the run drafts them* (PR7), cited by all three `BLOCK` escalation rules: straight after a BLOCKER's *Provide manual fix notes* answer, the run drafts that BLOCKER's notes from the finding's suggested fix and the artifact and asks `["Apply these notes (Recommended)", "Write my own notes (you'll be prompted)"]`, the draft in the question's own text; the plain-text prompt for the user's own notes says in its first line that the run waits for them. A BLOCKER whose fix needs a decision the run does not have, such as a `NEEDS HUMAN` one in `/document` or `/epics`, gets no draft: the run asks in plain text, saying first that it waits, then naming the decision and the finding's options, recommending none. §0's *A selection carries no text* names the picker as the one prompt a `(you'll be prompted)` option may lead to in place of the plain-text question. In `/design BOOK-1-01` and `/specify BOOK-1-02` the follow-up was a plain-text request that never said the run was waiting, and both users typed "done?" minutes later. How the notes are applied is unchanged.
- The references page describes the three rules.
- **`epic-picker` *The cap*: the remainder option names the Epics the array leaves out.** It is labelled `Another Epic (you'll be prompted for its key)`, and its `description` names every Epic the array leaves out — marker, key and title each, in the picker's order, after `Not shown as an option:` — since the dialog shows each option's description under its label. It read *Another Epic from the list above*, and run 1 of the live checks printed that list in none of eight pickers, so in the four that overflowed a left-out Epic was named nowhere. The prose list stays, as the place a greyed ● row reads best, but nothing depends on it now; a typed key is resolved against the array's rows and the description's. The worked example shows the label and the description (to-fix LC1).
- **`epic-picker` *The cap* states the `(Recommended)` marker rule**, which only its example carried, for the Epic pick alone: the first actionable row carries it, ◐ before ○, whether or not the cap applied; `/specify` recommends the broad PRD-level spec instead on a multi-component PRD with no PRD-level `specification.md`, and `/implement` recommends nothing wherever its broad-slice choice stands, in their one-Epic questions and pickers alike; and where every Epic row is ●, no Epic row carries it, the question's own text saying so, per `escalation-rules`' *When no option is safe to recommend*. Run 1 marked the first row in three of six `/implement` pickers and not in the other three (to-fix LC1).
- **`epic-picker` *The cap*: the description rule binds every picker built from a listing**, the file's second consumer set, not only the Epic picker: no option points at "the list above" or leans on a set named only in prose, the remainder option is labelled `Another <item> (you'll be prompted for …)` with a `description` naming every item the array leaves out, an option standing for a set or asking for names from it names every item of the set in its `description` — or the question's own text names the set, where several options turn on it, as the *overflow rule* does — and a typed answer is resolved against the items listed. A question over a set the run produced — a review's findings, a check's remaining failures, a read-back's follow-up tasks — names it as its own rule says, where the dialog shows it, and otherwise in the question's own text; a rule that names it only in prose printed before the prompt does not meet this. Its recipe for re-deriving both consumer sets now reads `references/` too, where `repo-resolution` sits (to-fix LC1).
- **`followup-emission` §7 numbers the preview's rows and names every one in the question's own text** — its number and its task line's action — so *approve-all* and *Select some* are answered over rows the dialog shows; a typed row number is resolved against the numbers listed (to-fix LC1).
- **`finding-triage`'s settle prompts name the verdict and each disposition they settle in the question's own text** — each survivor too, where any stands — one line each, where they were reported only in prose before the prompt (to-fix LC1).
- **`source-truth` §7.2's batch question names every discrepancy in its own text**, one line each, and no longer points at the table "above"; its three *for ALL* options act on every one (to-fix LC1).
- **`escalation-rules`' *Dirty working tree* names the repository and each dirty path in the question's own text**, read by the run, since `DIRTY_TREE` carries no paths (to-fix LC1).
- **`escalation-rules`' *When no option is safe to recommend* says so in the question's own text**, beside the list where the dialog shows it, never only in prose printed before the prompt; it said "in prose beside the list", which `epic-picker` *The cap* now contradicted (to-fix LC1).
- **`next-phase-offer`'s *overflow rule* puts its line that the list is longer than the prompt where the dialog shows it, naming every route the array leaves out**: in the question's own text, or, where an array carries an option naming the rest, in that option's `description`, as `epic-picker` *The cap* has it; no option's label sends the operator to the prose list. A run can skip the prose it prints before a prompt — run 1 of the live checks printed none in eight pickers — and a line there points at nothing (to-fix LC1).

### Fixed
- `phase-handoff` `handoff-to-main`: §2.5's push runs only where §2.4 made the commit (or §2.3 re-publishes one, below), as a shell command of its own run once the commit's exit status has been read — never chained after the commit, and never after a construct that swallows its failure (`rc=$?`, `|| true`, `;`) — and §2.6 is skipped with it. A live `/specify` handoff chained the switch, the add, the commit and the push in one command that kept the commit's status in `rc=$?`, and when a `pre-commit` hook refused the commit it still pushed an empty `spec/` branch at the default branch's tip. §4.1's *Commit failed* line now says that nothing was pushed and no pull request was opened, and its *Push failed* line gives the push to run, as *Push refused for access* does (to-fix LC2, live checks of 2026-10-26).
- `phase-handoff` §2.3 *Nothing to commit*: a branch §2.2 created in this run is removed — the run switches back to the branch it stood on, noted in §2.2 before the cut, and runs `branch -d`, never `-D` — so no empty branch is left and the run's session files land where they would have without a handoff. A reused branch is re-published where it carries a commit that is not a session-file commit and that neither `<default-ref>` nor `origin`'s copy of the branch holds — a deliverable an earlier handoff committed and could not push, or the operator's merge on row B′'s remedy — and otherwise both §2.5 and §2.6 are skipped. Where nothing is pushed, the §4.1 line says where the deliverable is: already on the default branch, on the branch unmerged (the three-dot diff from `<default-ref>` names a change of the branch's), or changed on the default branch after the branch was cut (row B′'s first finding) — on the default branch itself, changed on `<default-ref>` beyond a local copy that is behind it; `next-phase-offer`'s resolution table gives each form its own clause, naming the branch the run stands on and never the one it cut, where every *Nothing to commit* used to read "you can run it now". A cut branch git would not delete is named as holding no commit, for a later run on the default branch to delete; one git would not switch back off is named as the branch the run stays on. The *Branch name substituted* and *Stacked on another phase's branch* appends never describe a removed branch, §2.7 takes the pull-request title from the `title` input, which a re-publish has with no commit subject, and `next-phase-offer` no longer says a run that staged nothing opened no pull request without the re-publish exception (to-fix LC2).
- `specs-repo-git` `commit-artifacts`: §4 step 5 pushes only where step 4 made its commit, as a command of its own run once the commit's exit status has been read; §3.4's flush takes that precondition with the step, while its pushes of what an earlier run left — the retry, other plugin branches, the session branch — keep the step's three conditions alone (to-fix LC2).
- `specs-repo-git` `commit-artifacts`: §4 step 5 never creates a plugin branch on `origin`. A plugin branch `origin` does not have — no upstream there under its own name and no `refs/remotes/origin/<branch>` — keeps its session-file commits locally, with §6's new *`origin` does not have this plugin branch* line, until a later handoff of the branch pushes it or the operator does, the line naming the push; §3.4's retries inherit it. A handoff whose commit git refused used to leave such a branch, which the session-file push then put on `origin` with no deliverable and no pull request (to-fix LC2).
- The `references`, `/feedback` and `/prompt` pages say `commit-artifacts` does not push a branch the plugin created that `origin` does not have yet (to-fix LC2).
- **`next-phase-offer`'s *PE — breakdown & specification* lane names the stop `/dev-workflows:design` makes without a merged Epic `specification.md`**: on the PRD's address, Phase 0 step 4's excluded-count report; on an Epic's own address, `DESIGN_NO_SPEC` where no ref carries its `specification.md` and `require-on-main`'s stop where a plugin branch carries it. It said `DESIGN_NO_SPEC` for both, which the PRD's address never reaches (to-fix LC9).
- **`next-phase-offer`'s *PA* lane names both stops `/dev-workflows:design <EPIC>` makes without a merged `specification.md`**: `DESIGN_NO_SPEC` where no ref carries the Epic's `specification.md` (none written, or written and never committed), and `require-on-main`'s stop naming the branch or pull request where a plugin branch carries it. It said `DESIGN_NO_SPEC` for both (to-fix LC9).

## [1.59.1] — 2026-10-27

Take with `dev-workflows` 4.40.0 and `product-workflows` 3.48.0, whose *Set SPECS_PATH* sites this release's citer list names (batch B20).

### Fixed
- `escalation-rules` *Required path environment variable unset*: the citer list names `/conformance`, `/ready`, `/create-prd` and `/update-prd` for `SPECS_PATH`, and `/design`, `/specify`, `/create-ard` and `/epics` for `SPECS_PATH` as well as `REPOS_PATH`; its closing sentence no longer says other commands reproduce the list without naming the rule — none does now, and `scripts/check-docs.sh` check 23 holds that (to-fix D5, PRD-B1).
- `specs-repo-git` §3.1's unset-`$SPECS_PATH` gate no longer names `/epics` among the commands with no stop on the variable, nor a command reproducing the list inline (PRD-B2).

## [1.59.0] — 2026-10-26

Take with `dev-workflows` 4.39.2, `product-workflows` 3.47.0 and `docs-workflows` 1.25.0, whose questions this release's wording describes (batch B19).

### Changed
- `escalation-rules` §0, *A selection carries no text*: a two-option array of a text option and a real alternative — a *Cancel* the prose after it handles, a *Skip*, a way on without the text — is not "a picker built for" the text and stays; only an array with no real alternative is asked as a plain-text question. An alternative no prose handles is to be given its handling, not dropped; a plain-text question may still name its own way out, as *Branch prefix undetected*'s identity variant does (to-fix D13).
- `prd-format`: `/release-notes` takes the release from `--version` or its Phase 1 question, not "that same grill".
- `escalation-rules` *key dir not found*, *Required path environment variable unset* and *No repos derivable*: each now says what *Cancel* does — it ends the run, the stop standing — and *Required path environment variable unset* keeps a citer's own exception (`/brd-split`'s *Ground now*, where *Cancel* drops the continuation).
- `escalation-rules` *Closed-vocabulary pickers*: the missing-reason picker's *Cancel* returns the candidate to the four-option picker and adds no value.

### Fixed
- `followup-emission` §7: the follow-up preview's `select` option needed text without saying so; it is now `Select some (you'll be prompted for the row numbers)`, followed by one plain-text question for the rows (CORE-B6).

## [1.58.0] — 2026-10-26

Take with `product-workflows` 3.46.1, whose register stops state row B′'s new finding, and `dev-workflows` 4.39.1 (batch B01).

### Fixed
- **`specs-repo-git` §3.5: `branch-merged` finds a squash merge whose context drifted.** Tests 2 and 3 compare patch ids, which hash each hunk's context, so a squash landed after the default branch changed a line near one of the branch's hunks read unmerged for good: the next run stayed on the merged branch and its handoff recreated the branch the host had deleted. A fourth test asks whether merging the branch into the default branch changes nothing (`git merge-tree --write-tree`), gated on a non-empty change as test 3 is. Reproduced and verified in a throwaway repository (GIT-B1).
- **`specs-repo-git` §3.4: the flush no longer strands leftovers on a merged plugin branch.** Where HEAD is a plugin branch `branch-merged` finds merged, B2's switch runs first and the leftovers are committed on the default branch; where git refuses the switch, the flush commits on the branch as before and names the `cherry-pick` that lands it (GIT-B2).
- **`specs-repo-git` §3.4: merged local plugin branches are deleted.** The walk over the other plugin branches deletes one merged by ancestry with `branch -d` where git's own test can see the merge, printing `Specs preflight: deleted merged <branch>`, and otherwise — a rebase, a squash, its content, or ancestry `-d` cannot see from where the run stands — names the `branch -D` command once per run, so merged branches no longer pile up and force suffixes. A `Specs preflight: deleted merged <branch>` line settles a resume-pointer item waiting on that branch's merge (`session-hygiene` §1) (FBA-B2).
- **`specs-repo-git` §3.5: B2's and B4's pulls are `pull --ff-only --no-rebase`**, as the catch-up's is, so a `pull.rebase` setting cannot turn them into a rebase.
- **`phase-handoff` §2.2 rule 1: a remote-tracking ref the host deleted no longer forces a `-2` suffix.** A ref with no local branch behind it is confirmed with `ls-remote`, and treated as absent where the host lacks it (FBA-B1).
- **`phase-handoff` §3.3 row B′ also stops where both sides changed the path.** Row B passed a run's own branch that had amended the artifact even where the default branch changed it since the fork, so the run read a copy without the default branch's change. B′ now tests the default branch's change since the fork, and its finding says whether the branch changed the path too (D1). Its merge command is withheld under `specs_git: misrooted` and on a read-only mount, as row C's repair is (D2), and both its forms say the operator pushes the merge, or the next handoff does — the plain form adding that session commits wait until then (D14).
- **`phase-handoff` §3.6 quotes `specs-repo-git` §3.2's offline note exactly** (GIT-B5).

### Added
- **`phase-handoff` §4.1: a *Push refused for access* row**, naming the branch and the commit left behind and the exact `git push` to run once access is fixed, with its `next-phase-offer` `<merge-clause>` row (FBB-B30); and a consent choice asked again supersedes its earlier answer's line (FBA-B9).
- **`specs-repo-git` §6: a default-branch variant of the pushed line**, saying no pull request was opened and naming session-branch mode (FBB-B39).
- **`phase-handoff` §3.4: `/dev-workflows:conformance`'s row-F row**, which falls back (IMP-B3).
- **The `require-on-main` file names its own line count** in the line under its title, which `check-parts` holds fresh (GIT-G2).

D15 (row B′ on a git-ignored copy) stays open: no case was measured.
## [1.57.1] — 2026-10-26

Either version runs beside `dev-workflows` 4.39.0 or older: the change is to what this reference says of `/upgrade`.

### Fixed
- **`implementation-format` §3 said all three code-changing commands write the `[<key>]` convention.** `/upgrade` takes no address and is keyless, which `dev-workflows` 4.39.0 states; the table and the sentence below it now say it writes the subject with no key (batch B06, VU-B10's sweep).

## [1.57.0] — 2026-10-26

Take with `product-workflows` 3.46.0, whose consumers write `consumed_by` as the list and `baselines.md` in the layout this release fixes.

### Changed
- **`grounding-format` §2: `consumed_by` is a list each consumer adds to** — `none`, or `[PRD, ARD, specification]` in ladder order — never a value the next consumer overwrites. `/specify` replaced the ARD's stamp, so nine findings of a live run lost their record of the ARD drawing on them. A bare value already on file reads as a one-element list (FBB-B2).
- **`grounding-format` §3: a premise that restricts a surface the pinned commit does not have is `AMENDED`**, its correction "the surface does not exist at <commit>; the rule is a requirement on new work", where §2.2's control on the absence fires — `NOT-PROVABLE` where it fails — while a premise asserting the surface exists stays `REWRITTEN`. The grounder and the blind verifier had split such premises between `AMENDED` and `REWRITTEN` (FBA-B8).
- **`grounding-format` §4: one `baselines.md` entry layout** — `date`, `repo`, `commit`, `integrity`, `finding` — where each run improvised one that later runs parse (FBB-B40).
- **`pre-lint`:** the placeholder scan's `<…>` half skips fenced blocks and single-token inline code, so a design's code block can reach zero BLOCKERs, while a phrase slot in inline code (`` `<path or glob>` ``) and `TODO`-style tokens anywhere are still flagged; it says how to write a display format with a variable part (FBB-B3); the auto-link check gains a specs-tree branch — the hit extended to its whole key and resolved with `resolve-key`, a found key wrapped `[[KEY]]` mechanically, never left for author confirmation, an ambiguous one reported (FBB-B19); the PRD block names the fix for a story heading repeated under `## Acceptance Criteria` (FBB-B5); the spec block checks every criterion has a Happy-path and a Negative/boundary test case (FBB-B13 part 3); and the design block flags an unscoped `kubectl rollout restart`, `delete` or `scale`, a namespace flag before the verb included (FBA-G7).
- **`escalation-rules`' *Repo missing (after resolution)*** names the scanner's no-`origin` case beside a slug mismatch (CORE-B13).
- **`prd-format`:** acceptance criteria are grouped under a `**For [US#N] — <title>**` label line, never a repeated story heading (FBB-B5).
- **`code-scanner`:** it checks a directory exists before comparing its `origin` slug, and says when `REPO_MISSING` means "not the repository asked for", as the handoff now does (CORE-B13); it bounds every line number by the file's length, a coupling call's included, and drops one out of range (FBB-B18); and an optional `dependency_coupling` brief returns, per entry point, what it calls on its read and write paths (FBA-G5).

## [1.56.0] — 2026-10-26

**Take with `docs-workflows` 1.24.0** (`.claude/rules/plugin-updates-coupling.md`): `/document` carries its own copy of the per-discrepancy array changed below, and `/release-notes` presents it. Post-merge review 87.3, recorded as to-fix D23 and settled with the maintainer.

### Changed
- **`source-truth` §7.3 recommends *Document as actual (code)*, and *Document as intended (spec)* holds the release.** Published text, a documentation page or a release note alike, should describe what ships. Documenting the intended behaviour while the code lags drafts the bug report as before, and now the caller warns at once that the release and the publication of what the run wrote are held until the defect is fixed. It repeats the hold in its final report and on whatever carries the output toward publication (§7.5, *A `document-as-spec` gap holds the release*); the gap's *Docs status* says so too. §1 orders the choices to match, and §7.1's `SPEC-VS-PRD` row recommends neither where the code matches the spec, since both then write the same phrasing.
- **§7.7: a row whose code could not be read takes its own array**, recommending *Document as intended (spec)*, which there draws no marker, no gap and no hold, since nothing shows the code lagging; *Document as actual (code)* is left out, having nothing to write.

## [1.55.0] — 2026-10-26

Nothing here needs another plugin updated with it.

### Fixed
- **`test-notify` reports counts again.** It read `tool_response.output`, a key the Bash tool's result does not carry — the result is `stdout`, `stderr` and `interrupted` — so every test run notified `Test run: tests completed`, a red suite included. It now reads `stdout`, which carries the command's error output too, and `stderr` in case a harness keeps the two apart, and keeps `output` as a fallback (SCR-B1).
- **`test-notify` notifies a single Gradle module's tests**, `./gradlew :<module>:test`, the form `dev-workflows`' `/vuln` and `/upgrade` references prescribe, which matched none of its entries. The new entry is a pattern — a whole argument ending in `:test`, after any flags or other tasks — so `./gradlew -q :api:test` matches and `:api:testClasses` does not (CORE-B14).
- **`/prompt-brainstorm` checks for `superpowers:brainstorming` before it logs.** It recorded "Handed off to superpowers:brainstorming" and then handed off to a skill that might not be installed, and `dependencies` promised an embedded fallback technique that does not exist. It tests the session's skill listing, since invoking the skill to probe it would cede the session. Where the skill is absent, the Resolution now says the correction was not explored, Phase 3 invokes nothing and names `/prompt-grill-me` and `/prompt`, and `dependencies` says so (CORE-B5).
- **A keyless feedback entry's `id` starts with `NOKEY`**, the form already in use; `feedback-emission` §1 named no key segment for a run with none, so two runs could log one signal under two ids (CORE-B8).
- **`model-routing/classification` §4 names the two research agents** — `vuln-research` and `upgrade-planner` — where "Both agents this parenthetical names" pointed at a parenthetical naming steps (CORE-B12).
- **`read-only-repos` gives the recipe for its consumers** instead of a list of five that was short of its rules file by two agents and several commands (GIT-B7).

## [1.54.1] — 2026-10-26

### Removed
- **`obsidian-llm-wiki` left the marketplace**, so `scripts/command-namespaces.json` no longer lists its ten commands, `dependencies` names three siblings rather than four, and `followup-emission` §5 and §7 no longer point at its `/wiki-tasks-extract` — each states its rule on its own (batch B15).

## [1.54.0] — 2026-10-26

Finding 83.1 (MAJOR) and NIT 82.5 of the 2026-10-26 post-merge review of #82–#85 and #87. Either version runs beside any `product-workflows` from 3.42.0, whose `/brd-reconcile` already takes the third exception below; this release stops the reference contradicting it.

### Fixed
- **`phase-handoff` §3.7 said `/brd-reconcile` reads a stopping row without stopping at two sites, "never at its ordinary Phase 0 gate", and that no other caller may take either exception.** Since `product-workflows` 3.42.0 that gate, run on the folder's most recent prompt while the package the review answers is undetermined, tests each older prompt, newest first, and stops on the most recent prompt's return only where none passes. A run loaded both instructions, and following this one re-created BRD3-B19. §3.7 now names that gate as the third site, and the generated `reference-parts/phase-handoff/require-on-main.md` with it. (83.1)
- **1.49.0's entry said the proposal profile's producer set holds "a stronger class beside"**; for `/brd-proposal` the umbrella `proposal.md` is advisory, the profile's own class, so it now reads "an equal or stronger class". (82.5)

## [1.53.0] — 2026-10-26

**Take with `dev-workflows` 4.36.0 and `docs-workflows` 1.21.0**: `/dev-workflows:conformance`'s PRD run fetches each Epic's branch for the merged test below from 4.35.0 on, and `/docs-workflows:release-notes` reads the note boundary below from 1.21.0 on. Each states what it needs beside its citation, so an older one still runs. Closes to-fix C2 and C9, and DOC-B-B1 of the 2026-10-26 bugs review.

### Changed
- **`implementation-format` §5's merged test has three legs, and its subject leg is corroborated by content.** A default-branch commit carrying `[<key>]` used to count as the merge whatever it held, so a teammate's commit following §3's convention, or an earlier run's pull request merging after a later block, read an open pull request as merged. That leg now counts only where the block's recorded commit, or the branch's tip, adds nothing to that commit or to `<ref>` (the existing `merge-tree` content test). It fails only where every call adds work cleanly; a conflict — a squash whose review commits rewrote what the recorded commit wrote — leaves it passing on the subject alone, as does a call that compared nothing or a clone holding neither commit nor tip. Two misses that err toward merged are named: a clone holding neither, and an open pull request whose work conflicts with the key-carrying commit (to-fix C10). The content leg — a tip that adds nothing to `<ref>`, a squash whose subject lost the key — moves from `unit-branch` step 2 into the merged test itself, so `/conformance`'s PRD run, which reads that test, sees the merge `unit-branch` sees (C9). `unit-branch`'s states and `after_merge` are read from the same calls, never run twice. (C2, C9)
- **§4's note boundary: two kinds of note bound nothing** — one marked superseded, and, on a run filing under `# Unreleased`, every note there for the run's own key, which it supersedes. A re-drafted note used to read only what changed since the note it replaced, while that note, marked superseded, still bounded every later run. (DOC-B-B1)
- **§1: a run that committed nothing writes no block** (`dev-workflows` 4.36.0's `/implement`), and a `commit: null` block an earlier one wrote may be deleted by hand. (IMP-B2)
- **`security-register-format` §5** calls the register's squash leg the shape of the merged test's subject leg, held to the sighting instead of corroborated by content.

## [1.52.0] — 2026-10-26

Fix batch B16 of the 2026-10-26 review: `escalation-rules`, `next-phase-offer` and `epic-picker`. Install it with `product-workflows` 3.43.0 and `dev-workflows` 4.35.0 (`.claude/rules/plugin-updates.md`).

### Changed
- **`next-phase-offer`'s `<merge-clause>` table resolves `phase-handoff` §4.1's *Commit failed*** to `(once the deliverable staged on <branch> is committed, pushed and its pull request merged)`, where it fell to the catch-all, which named no branch. The paragraph under the table names all four appends and resolves every outcome row of §4.1 that is not an append, counting none, and the rows that name a branch are those that left something on one — committed or staged.
- **`epic-picker`'s *The cap*: the array carries every Epic where the Epics and the command's own option fit in four**, and the cap — at most three Epic rows plus the remainder option, fewer beside a command's own option — applies only otherwise. Its example now lists five Epics, where it listed four under the cap that the rule two lines later said four need not take.
- **The BRD lane offers `/product-workflows:brd-reconcile <BRD-KEY> [@<review-file>]`** after `/product-workflows:brd-package`, the path optional, as `/brd-package`'s own `<review-clause>` is: on the repository route the review is committed beside the bundle and `/brd-reconcile` takes no path.
- **The PE lane's `/dev-workflows:design <PRD>` on a PRD holding Epics** is offered only where an Epic's `specification.md` is on the default branch, as the PA lane states for `/dev-workflows:design <EPIC>`: `/dev-workflows:design` stops with `DESIGN_NO_SPEC` without one.
- **`escalation-rules`' version line names by id what an edit after the verdict changed in substance** — an `[AD#N]` decision or its Rule, a contract row, a criterion or test case, a PRD's `[FR#N]` or `[AC#N]` — as *"Changed in substance since: <each id>"*, an edit to text with no id naming its section, so a pull-request reviewer need not diff the whole artifact.
- **`escalation-rules`' offer to apply what a review leaves open names `/epics`** among its users, its edits the orchestrator's own since `doc-fixer` takes no `NIT`, and *An inline fix is read against what it overlaps* names it at those edits.

## [1.51.0] — 2026-10-26

**Update `dev-workflows` to 4.34.0 and `product-workflows` to 3.42.0 with this release**: their commands cite the rule below by name. Closes to-fix PR1, and FBB-B13 of the 2026-10-26 bugs review.

### Changed
- **`grilling-technique`'s overlap read lists what a requirement reaches, not only what its wording meets.** A new *What a requirement overlaps* names five kinds, each listed before it is read: its condition (the trigger, precondition, state or figure the read compared before), what it imports and what imports it, its trigger's other outcomes, its place on a path — both ways, so a step placed ahead of a failure's answer is read against it whichever was written first — and the inherited `[AD#N]` Rules. The kinds are listed in the run's own working, never in the artifact. The read before each play-back now covers stories, scope lines and decisions beside criteria, test cases and Rules. Four live misses came in through those links: a criterion that carried a `PUT`-only rule to `POST` by applying "every rule of a change", a new outcome that left its sibling criteria on the old set, security hooks placed ahead of a database-down answer they pre-empted, and a fix that accepted missing an `[AD#N]` bound with no deviation recorded.
- **`escalation-rules`' *An inline fix is read against what it overlaps*** reads each requirement an edit adds or changes against the same five kinds, citing that definition, where it read only those whose trigger, precondition, state or figure overlapped; its lead sentence says so, with the inherited `[AD#N]` Rules beside the artifact. `reference-parts/escalation-rules/review.md` is regenerated.

### Fixed
- `phase-handoff` §3.4's `/brd-reconcile` row names the prompt of the package the review answers, not the most recent one, as `product-workflows` 3.40.0 now gates it.

## [1.49.0] — 2026-10-26

- `phase-handoff` §4.0 classifies `.dev-workflows/proposal-profile.yml` as advisory, and §2.9's `deliverable_paths` row says the proposal commands declare it on every run: `product-workflows` 3.39.0's proposal commands now hand it off with their proposal. `addressing` §5 notes that `/prd-proposal`'s stop on a pre-rename PRD names the rename. Either version runs beside the other — the row only names the reader of a path the producer's set already holds an equal or stronger class beside.

## [1.48.0] — 2026-10-26

**Update `product-workflows` to 3.38.1 with this release**: the lanes, the picker rule and the deliverable table below state what its `/create-ard` and `/specify` do since 3.37.0 and 3.38.0. Closes to-fix D17.

### Fixed
- **`next-phase-offer`'s `pa` lane** offers `/dev-workflows:design <EPIC>` after an Epic-level `/create-ard` only where the Epic's `specification.md` is on the default branch, as `/create-ard` does, since `/design` stops with `DESIGN_NO_SPEC` without one; its **`pe` lane** adds `/dev-workflows:design <PRD>` after a PRD-level `/specify`, to design the spec whole, on a multi-component PRD only as `/design`'s override where the PRD folder holds no Epic folder, and as designing those Epics where it does.
- **`epic-picker`'s ● row** does what the command's own ● rule says, rather than always offering to revise: `/specify` rewrites behind an archive and stops on a published specification.

## [1.47.0] — 2026-10-26

**Update `security-workflows` to 0.4.0 with this release**: `security-register-format` states what its `register.py` now does. Batch B04 of the 2026-10-26 review.

### Changed
- **`security-register-format`** says how a decisions file's numbers reach the plan a run commits against — `commit --answered-plan` and `rebase` translate them through each finding's origin (§2) — that `fixing` and `reopened` are `register.py`'s own, that `fixed` is confirmed only where a scan proposes the closure, and that `commit` refuses an empty `--by` (§4), and that a follow-up line carries a fixed clause where its patch was verified on review only (§3).

## [1.46.0] — 2026-10-26

The cost path finds this run's own transcript, under the folder name Claude Code actually uses, and the cost and feedback ladders find a kind-prefixed folder. Fixes batch B02 of the 2026-10-26 review (`docs/superpowers/reviews/2026-10-26-fix-batches.md`). No dependent plugin changes with it.

### Fixed
- **A run finds its own transcript, not the newest one.** `cost-emission` §1 and its restated copy in `run-flags` §5 (`skip-cost`) took the newest `*.jsonl` in the project folder as the current session; with two sessions open in one working directory, which `CLAUDE.md` § Git allows, a run billed the other session's window, advanced its checkpoint and, under `--skip-costs`, dropped its deferred claims. The main transcript is now the one holding this run's own invocation as the newest such record of any transcript there — the `<command-name>` record with its arguments, or, for a command started through the Skill tool (`/brd-split`'s *Ground now* running `/product-workflows:prd-ground`), the `Skill` `tool_use` naming it with the same `input.args` — so a session that wrote last, or ran the same command earlier, is not taken for this run's; where none holds it, the cost phase prints `cost not measured — …`, writes no entry and advances no checkpoint, and `skip-cost` advances none (CORE-B1).
- **`<cwd-slug>` replaces every character outside `[A-Za-z0-9]` with `-`**, not only `/` and `.`: Claude Code stores a session run in `…/slug_t x@y.z` under `…-slug-t-x-y-z`, checked live. A working directory holding `_`, a space or `@` found no transcript and wrote no cost entry. Where no folder of the derived name exists, the folder whose transcripts' `cwd` equals the working directory is used. Fixed in `cost-emission` §1, `run-flags` §5 and `session-diagnosis` §2 (CORE-B2).
- **Tier 1 of the cost and feedback ladders finds a kind-prefixed folder.** `cost-emission` §8 and `feedback-emission` §2 matched the PRD folder by §5's legacy name `<KEY>{-|_}<slug>` alone, so read literally a `PRD-`/`BRD-` folder fell to the pending or unfiled tier. Tier 1 now takes the folder the run's own `resolve-address` found, or the one `resolve-key` returns as `found`, placed by `addressing` §4.1, with `absent` or `ambiguous` read as no match, never a stop (CORE-B7).
- `cost-emission` §6.1 cites §8 tier 4 for report-only, not a tier 5 that does not exist (CORE-B4); §11 and §13 point at §7, which holds the twenty-eight commands that measure themselves, not §1 (CORE-B9); §5 drops a "pending check" the live measurement beside it had already settled (CORE-B10).
- **`cost-emission` §12, the model-onboarding checklist,** places a new Haiku id in `run-flags` §2's Haiku rows, which no `classification.md` chain replaces, adds a step for every site naming the superseded chain head with the `grep` that finds them, and rewrites a garbled sentence (CORE-B3).
- `cost-prices.yaml`'s comments quote the pricing page's rule for which tokens count toward prompt length, and name Mythos 5.1 beside Fable 5.1 among the 0.025x cache-read exceptions (CORE-B11).
- `specs-repo-git` §4's account of the emitter tail and the session-cost reference page name the new no-transcript case.

## [1.45.0] — 2026-10-24

**Update `dev-workflows` to 4.31.0, `product-workflows` to 3.36.0, `docs-workflows` to 1.18.0 and `security-workflows` to 0.3.0 with this release**: each of the first three cites `phase-handoff` §2.6.1's `owner-repo` — `dev-workflows`' `code-handoff`, `docs-workflows`' `finish-and-handoff` and `key-discovery` for its SSH-alias rule, `/product-workflows:harvest-decisions` for its derivation of `OWNER_REPO` — a section this release cuts out of §2.6, so beside 1.44.0 a run looks for a section that reference does not have and meets the rule only in §2.6. And `dev-workflows` 4.31.0's `/dev-workflows:implement` and `/dev-workflows:conformance` and `security-workflows` 0.3.0's `/security-workflows:scan` name the other Epics of the PRD by key and order them with this Epic's pull request by the ARD's landing order, citing `next-phase-offer`'s *The remaining Epics are named*, which 1.44.0 does not have, while 1.44.0's lane lines for the three write "the remaining Epics" bare — for `/conformance` and `/scan`, after this Epic's merge — so beside 1.44.0 a run meets two instructions that disagree. Everything else they change runs beside 1.44.0, which already serves `escalation-rules core` and reads the whole reference for `args: "phase-handoff require-on-main"`; `product-workflows` 3.36.0's `/product-workflows:prd-ground`, its three grounding agents, `bundle-packaging` §1.2 and `/product-workflows:brd-package`'s stop also cite `grounding-format` §2.3 for keeping the operator's model and machine out of a grounding file, which 1.44.0's §2.3 does not say, but each states the rule itself, so it runs there all the same — and its `code-grounder` and `grounding-verifier` cite §3's *A claim that restricts a value*, a subsection 1.44.0 does not have, but state the rule's core beside the citation, as they state that an absence read off a code path owes a control, so they run there too; and `dev-workflows` 4.31.0's `/dev-workflows:design` cites `grilling-technique` for the three rules this release adds to it, which 1.44.0's does not carry, but states each itself too; and `/dev-workflows:implement`, `/docs-workflows:document` and `/product-workflows:epics` cite `specs-repo-git` §4 step 7 for what may follow a report printed before the tail, which 1.44.0's step 7 does not say, but each states the rule itself, so it runs there all the same. `security-workflows` 0.3.0's question-rules step needs nothing of this release, and neither does its `/security-workflows:scan` naming a worktree it failed to remove in its report and its feedback, never in the resume pointer, nor its naming the register's slug beside each Security Engineer offer, which 1.44.0's §1, overwriting the pointer outright, never read — though beside 1.44.0 its list writes that slug where 1.44.0's dev lane writes the offer bare, two live instructions that disagree in that pairing, at no cost there, since that §1 reads neither, and its account of how §1 carries and settles the offer is true of this release's §1 alone. The reverse loses no rule — `grilling-technique`'s three new rules bind every grilling caller whatever its version, and contradict none of them; `grounding-format`'s §3 rule for a claim that restricts a value and §2.2's control on an absence however it was reached bind an older `code-grounder` and `grounding-verifier`, which load the reference whole, and contradict neither; and `session-hygiene` §1's carried items and `specs-repo-git` §4 step 7's rule for what follows a report bind every writer of a pointer and every caller of `commit-artifacts` whatever its version, and, save §1's bar on a path outside every repository the pointer's readers share (below), contradict none of them, an older `/dev-workflows:implement`, `/docs-workflows:document` or `/product-workflows:epics` calling its report the final output while its own terminal phase prints the cost and `Specs repo:` lines after it, which step 7 lets follow the report — and mostly reads less: an older dependent's `require-on-main` request reads the entry point's own file here too, and a later citation of a section the file holds works from it. Four read more, as this release's `check-citations`, run over the dependents as they stood beside 1.44.0, measures it: an older `/product-workflows:create-prd`, `/product-workflows:prd-ground` and `/product-workflows:brd-package`, whose Phase 0 cites a section the file does not hold after the run has read the file, and an older `/dev-workflows:conformance` where no ARD resolves, whose Phase 1 cites §4.3 before its handoff loads the whole reference, each read the whole reference after the file — about 69k characters more than under 1.44.0, where the request read the whole reference once, beside the loader's entry-point rules, about 2k, which every run requesting `phase-handoff` reads once under this release. The reverse misleads in four places. Beside an older `dev-workflows` or `security-workflows`, `next-phase-offer` has `/dev-workflows:implement`'s, `/dev-workflows:conformance`'s and `/security-workflows:scan`'s next step name each remaining Epic by key and place it by the ARD's landing order, while the older commands write "the remaining Epics" bare — `/implement` with a `<SIBLING-EPIC>` placeholder, `/conformance` and `/scan` after this Epic's merge — so the two disagree in that pairing; update `dev-workflows` and `security-workflows` with it. And `grounding-format` §2.3: beside an older `product-workflows` it tells `/product-workflows:prd-ground` that its Phase 8 replaces this machine's paths by their literal value and that the tier is in its Final report alone, neither of which the older command does; it bars a checkout's path from a grounding file where the older grounding agents keep each command they ran as they ran it, so the two instructions disagree in that pairing; and it says `/product-workflows:brd-package` puts a model id in the customer's naming a finding quotes to the operator's ruling, where the older one has no model-id row and ships every model id unremarked. Update `product-workflows` with it. And `session-hygiene` §1 never writes into the resume pointer a path outside every repository the pointer's readers share, while an older `/security-workflows:scan` names there a worktree it failed to remove, so the two instructions disagree in that pairing — though whichever the run follows, the next run's §1 settles that item at once, as 1.44.0's next run overwrote it; the `security-workflows` update above covers it. And beside an older `/security-workflows:scan`, whose Security Engineer offers name no register slug where this release's dev lane writes one beside each, §1 settles an offer that names one on any machine and carries one that names none until a run on a machine where its path is that repository derives the slug — a case §1 lists among those that stay longer — whichever of the two the run follows.

A consumer's Phase-0 `require-on-main` check reads about half of `phase-handoff` instead of all of it, every command that presents a choice list loads the rules its questions follow before it asks one, and a run that already holds the loader's text makes a load's call and first reads in one turn.

### Added
- **`require-on-main` reads its own file.** `reference-parts/phase-handoff/require-on-main.md`, generated from markers in `phase-handoff` and never edited by hand, holds the entry point and every rule its check applies: the text before §1, §1 — rule 5, *`require-on-main` is fatal by design*, which §3.7 relies on — §2.2, which `/dev-workflows:conformance`'s and `/product-workflows:brd-reconcile`'s checks apply, §2.6.1, §3 with its §3.4 table whole, §4.4's stop contract, §4.0's *Never infer* rule, and §5's rules 2 and 3 — about 69k of the reference's 141k characters, 49%. Its title line names what it holds. **The measured reason:** in lean context part 1's seven live runs `phase-handoff` cost $5.59 in carried reads, because it was read whole at Phase 0 and carried by every later call of the run, and a read early in a run costs its size again on every call after it. The handoff phase — the consent choice and `handoff-to-main` — needs about 82% of the file and runs late, so it keeps reading the whole reference; `specs-repo-git`, `addressing`, `cost-emission`, `run-flags` and `model-routing/classification` were measured and are not split.
- **The loader serves that file, and nothing else changes what it reads of the reference.** `args: "phase-handoff require-on-main"` reads the file and executes the entry point from it. A citation of a section the file holds — a section it holds in part only for the text its quoted phrase names — is a lookup the file serves: wherever the file's text, or the whole reference's, is in the conversation from a read made in the run, in any phase, the citation works from it and reads nothing, so a phase between the check and the handoff cites a section the file holds at no cost. Where it is not, the citation reads the file inside a `##` section that also requests the entry point, before the request or after it — that section reads the file at most once — and the whole reference anywhere else. Every other request reads the whole reference, as before: `args: "phase-handoff"`, `args: "phase-handoff handoff-to-main"`, and a citation of a section the file does not hold or of no section. A read of the whole reference covers the file, within the usual bound on reuse. The loader keeps these rules in its entry-point rules, a file beside it, `skills/reference/entry-files.md`, about 2k characters, which a request on `phase-handoff`, with or without a second argument, reads with or before its other reads, unless the run already holds it word for word; the loader's own text, which comes with every request, names only the entry point, its file's path and the rules file, so a run that never requests `phase-handoff` carries none of them.
- **`/workflows-core:frames`, `/workflows-core:statusline`, `/workflows-core:feedback`, `/workflows-core:prompt`, `/workflows-core:prompt-brainstorm` and `/workflows-core:prompt-grill-me` load the question rules as a step of their own.** Each carries a **Load the question rules.** step — `escalation-rules core`, about 9.9k characters: §0's array shape, choice lists presented verbatim, the `(Recommended)` marker and a selection that carries no text — after its run flags are stripped and before its first question; `/frames`' first is its *Required path* question, ahead of `phase-handoff` §4.3's consent choice, and each of the other four's first choice list is a prompt of 2–4 options it builds at run time, with no `choices:` array of its own — `/feedback`'s grouped confirmation of the note's metadata, asked after a free-text question for the note itself where the run was given none, and the others' question naming the command being corrected. **The measured reason:** in part 1's live runs of `/security-workflows:scan` and `/dev-workflows:design` the run asked questions without ever loading core, as runs of both did before part 1: each command cited it inside a rule statement, which a run reads as a source note rather than a step. `/frames` presented §4.3's array, and the four feedback commands their prompts, with no citation of core at all.
- **`phase-handoff` §2.6.1, `owner-repo`**, the derivation of `$OWNER_REPO` as a block of its own, which §2.6's pull-request calls and §3.5's probe both name; `args: "phase-handoff owner-repo"` is an entry-point request now, which reads the whole reference.

### Changed
- **`phase-handoff` names what its check applies**, so the file holds those rules in words a run meets there: §2.6 derives `$OWNER_REPO` first and then probes for a pull request, where the probe used the value ahead of the paragraph deriving it; §3's opening names the rules of `specs-repo-git` and `read-only-repos` it applies and says it looks them up — any read of them in the run serves, and each is loaded whole only where none is in the conversation, which adds no read to `/dev-workflows:ready`, `/product-workflows:epics`, `/product-workflows:specify`'s gate on an Epic its Step A picks, `/product-workflows:brd-proposal` or `/product-workflows:brd-reconcile`'s cross-BRD write guard, whose checks run a phase or more after the preflight; §3.3 says every stop it makes is worded by §4.4's stop contract; and §5's header says whom each of its four rules binds.
- **`phase-handoff` §5 rule 4 has one carve-out from citing by section number**, in a file that requests `require-on-main`'s own file, where a citation of the reference can load the whole of it beside that file: a pointer that only explains names the contract in words — inside a `##` section that requests the file, one to text the file does not hold (a section it does not hold, one it holds in part for other text than the rules its title line quotes, or the reference by no section), unless a whole load of the reference — `args: "phase-handoff"`, or a request of an entry point with no file of its own — stands at or before it in that section; before the first such section in the citing file, any pointer into the reference, one to a section the file holds included, unless its own sentence loads the whole reference; and after that section, before the citing file's first load of the whole reference, one to text the file does not hold, unless its own sentence loads the whole reference. A step that applies such a section's rule still cites it by number, with the whole load its case asks for.
- **`specs-repo-git` §2.1's docs-repository slug and `architecture-promotion`'s `superseding` set** derive `OWNER_REPO` by §2.6.1's `owner-repo`, where they named §2.6.
- **`grilling-technique` reads a premise about the existing system before a question states it.** A question, an option or a recommendation's rationale that states how the system already behaves — a precedent, what a script, a build or a job produces, a count, how an existing endpoint answers, what a deploy step changes — is read in the repository that holds it, at the ref the caller's scan read, or in a grounding finding whose verification agreed with it or extended it, across every instance it generalises over; an option the read falsifies is rewritten or dropped, and one the run can read nowhere is stated as unverified. The play-back marks what the run inferred about the user's intent and decisions, as before, and gives a statement about the existing system with the line it was read at, or marked unverified, never as an inference for the user to confirm. And an inherited `[AD#N]` Rule is read word for word before a recommendation of the outcome it assigns to an answer or a state, as before one of a bound, a match rule, a limit or an enumeration. The references page says so.
