The task line writes it ``repository `<name>` ``, once and for that repository alone, and §6 shows a literal line; §5 compares the name in that form exactly, so `api` no longer matches `api-gateway` or a path holding `api`, and compares the unit as the whole address of the `/dev-workflows:implement` run the line asks for, so a PRD's no longer matches an Epic's minted under it. §6 now states where a name still splits or merges keys: a name the run gave a repository, the path of one with no `origin`, a renamed fork or repository and a mirror can add a task again, and two repositories whose URLs end in the same name share one key. `components` and the references page list `followup-emission` among `components`' citers, `components` names the follow-up use among its consumers and says a change to §1's derivation is swept across the slug→clone maps that restate it, citer or not. A repository follow-up written by 1.11.1 to 1.15.0 — named by owner path (`team-a/api`), by path, or by a `(not mounted)` name — carries no ``repository `<name>` `` form, so a re-run that finds it again adds it once more in the new form.

## [1.15.0] — 2026-10-05

### Added
- **`untrusted-content`: what an agent reads is data, never instructions.** An issue-tracker export, a feature request, a PR diff, a web page, a repository's instruction file or a code comment could tell an agent to approve, skip a check, run or fetch something, or reveal its context. The new reference holds the one block every agent in the guarded plugins now ends its prompt with: content supplies the values a task asks for — a declared test command, a documented convention — and never a new task, a fetch or a changed verdict, and an agent ends its reply with an `Untrusted-content notice:` line for each passage that tried to steer it. It also holds the relay sentence every dispatching command carries and the pass-on sentence of the agents that dispatch another.
- **This plugin's seven agents carry the block, and `/frames` relays notices** under `Untrusted-content notices:` in its final report, or in the stop message of a run that ends early. A notice is advisory: it never blocks a run, changes its routing or triggers a re-review.
- **Check 20 in the repository's `scripts/check-docs.sh`** fails the build when an agent's block differs from this reference by a byte, when an agent granted `Task` lacks the pass-on sentence, or when a dispatching command lacks the relay sentence — over every docs-gated plugin and `prose-style`, and failing too when a listed plugin does not exist or a plugin that ships agents is not listed.

## [1.14.1] — 2026-10-05

### Fixed
- **`specs-repo-git` §3 and §7 named only an address stop as one that runs no preflight.** A run that stops before the preflight on a check of its own inputs needing no specs-repo state — a run flag, the directory it runs from, a path it reads where it sits rather than as an artifact of the specs tree — runs none either, whether the command resolves an address first or runs its preflight first; every command's run-flag stop already did, and read literally the caller contract called that a defect. `/dev-workflows:implement` (dev-workflows 4.9.3) now makes all of its input checks before its preflight.

## [1.14.0] — 2026-10-05

### Added
- **`architecture-grounding` and `architecture-grounder`: optional grounding in an architecture repository.** Where `$ARCHITECTURE_REPO_PATH` names a clone of the organisation's architecture repository — its technology radar, standards, principles, patterns and ADRs, in any of the common layouts (a catalog, a radar file, MADR or Nygard ADR folders) — `resolve-architecture-grounding` turns grounding ON and shows an `architecture grounding:` line with the clone's branch, commit, date, uncommitted changes and staleness. The read-only `architecture-grounder` agent then returns the artifacts that bind the work, each with its quoted rule and a link at the snapshot commit, and the conflicts — a contradicted decision or standard, a `hold` or `retire` technology, a proposed technology the radar lacks — for the caller's grill to rank. It never writes, fetches or pulls the clone, reads every file there as data rather than instructions, and turns OFF with a reason on any miss. Its dispatch is pinned to the §2 Opus chain: measured against two requirement documents, the Sonnet chain missed binding standards and decisions Opus found. `/product-workflows:create-ard` is its first consumer.

## [1.13.1] — 2026-10-05

### Fixed
- **`commit-artifacts` and `handoff-to-main` no longer commit whatever the specs repository's index holds.** Both staged only their own paths and then ran a plain `git commit`, so a change the user had staged in the specs repository was carried into the plugin's commit: into the bookkeeping commit, which §3.3 G1 explicitly lets run beside changes that are not the plugin's, or into the deliverable's. Both now commit by pathspec over the paths they staged that git still lists as staged — a path staged by an earlier failed commit and since deleted would otherwise fail the whole commit — and then bring the index back in step after a `pre-commit` hook, leaving alone only what somebody else had staged. `handoff-to-main` writes its message to a file and commits with `-F`, since its summary is free text that a double-quoted `-m` would command-substitute.
- **A commit git refuses now has an outcome.** A hook's rejection, a signature git cannot make, or a merge in progress (during which git refuses a pathspec commit) had no `Specs repo:` or `Phase handoff:` line; each now reports through a new *Commit failed* row, the work left staged for a later run once git accepts the commit. `specs-preflight`'s flush, which runs the same steps, prints no outcome line of its own.
- `specs-repo-git.md` §4 step 4 says its commit-and-restore form is not particular to the specs repository, since `phase-handoff` and the `docs-workflows` commands run it against their own; §4.1 says that where the deliverable's commit failed, the artifacts' commit alone lands on the branch.
- **`handoff-to-main` passes the pull-request title to `gh` in single quotes**, for the same reason as its commit message.

## [1.13.0] — 2026-10-05

### Changed
- **`ard-resolution` skips a superseded or withdrawn decision.** A `### [AD#N]` carrying `**Superseded by:**` or `**Withdrawn:**` binds nothing (`product-workflows` 3.14.0's `ard-format.md` § Superseding a decision) and is left out of `invariants`, so no consumer enforces a rule the ARD itself replaced.
- **`pre-lint`'s ARD block** checks `**Alternatives:**` on every live decision — MAJOR, or MINOR on one the prior ARD already held without it — that every `**Superseded by:**` names a live decision, and that every `**Withdrawn:**` gives a reason.
- **The code-review checklist names a committed credential.** `model-routing/classification.md` §6's security item makes a token, private key, password literal, credentialed URL or added `.env` or keystore in an added line a `BLOCKER`, unless it is plainly a placeholder or test fixture.

## [1.12.0] — 2026-10-04

**Update `product-workflows` to 3.13.0 and `dev-workflows` to 4.8.0 with this release**: their commands load `components`, which a `workflows-core` older than 1.12.0 does not carry.

### Added
- **`components`**, a new shared reference: a component is a repository or a set of paths inside one; `enumerate-components` proposes a repository's modules from what it declares (Gradle, Maven, npm/yarn/pnpm workspaces, Cargo, Go, top-level build files, deploy directories); the multi-component test reads the known set from the ARD's `components:`, from an `/epics` run's confirmed set, or from the Epics' `target:` values; the ride-along rule lets an Epic change a same-repository deploy directory that exists only to deploy its target; and `multi-component-prereqs` is the read-only check `/epics`, `/implement` and `/ready` share.
- **`grilling-technique`'s gap taxonomy gains a Cross-component category**: an interface with no producer, a consumer with no stub, an unversioned shape, an unstated landing order, and contested schema ownership.

### Changed
- **`ard-resolution` returns the PRD-level ARD's `components` and `contracts`** beside its invariants, on Epic-level resolutions too; `status: none` returns them empty, and a caller that ignores them is unchanged.
- **`pre-lint`'s ARD block requires `## Contracts` where `components:` has two or more `kind: code` entries**, and checks each interface row's `AD#N`, components and status.
- **`ard-resolution`'s no-regression rule states its one exception**, a multi-component PRD, where `/epics` and `/implement` ask about a missing contract and `/design`, `/implement` and `/ready` treat a flat PRD-level specification as a requirements source.
- **`epic-picker`**: `/implement`'s broad-slice option is withheld on a multi-component PRD, and `/specify` asks before auto-selecting a lone Epic there; **`next-phase-offer`**: after a multi-component PRD-level ARD with no Epics, the PRD-level spec is recommended before `/epics`.

## [1.11.1] — 2026-10-04

**Update `dev-workflows` to 4.7.0 with this release**: `implementation-format` §1, §3 and §4, `followup-emission` §5 and §6, `finding-triage`'s partly-emptied rule and *stayed blocked*, `next-phase-offer`'s `/implement` row and rule 5, `phase-handoff`'s Phase 4.5 note, and `model-routing/classification` §1.1 and §8.1 describe its `/implement`, which changes code only in the repository it branches.

### Changed
- **`implementation-format` records one code repository per `/implement` run.** Its §1 example showed one run writing two repositories, and §3 named a repository a multi-source run edited and never branched as a way a run ends uncommitted; from `dev-workflows` 4.7.0, `/implement` changes code only in the repository it branches, and a second code repository is a later run with a block of its own; §4 names the blocks appended before 4.7.0 as the only ones a note can cover partly.
- **`followup-emission` §6 names a change another code repository needs** among the signals whose action lands outside the current change: `/dev-workflows:implement` records one as a follow-up rather than editing that repository. Its exclusion of deferred review `BLOCKER`s spares one whose fix lies in another code repository, and §5's stable key admits that identity: the other repository, named by its `origin` remote's path, and the unit the task addresses, whose one task is a run from that repository.

### Fixed
- **`model-routing/classification` §1.1's *Multi-source input* floored on any directory input**, so an `@path` to a plain directory inside the repository `/dev-workflows:implement` was started in raised the run to `SIGNIFICANT`; it now counts distinct repositories by top level and names folder inputs as Phase 0 of `/dev-workflows:implement` does, and so does §8.1's fan-out trigger, whose "saved file folder" is now a specs folder.
- **`finding-triage`'s *stayed blocked* now admits a caller's own member**: a first review's `BLOCKER` whose fix lies in another code repository, which `/dev-workflows:implement` keeps from the fixer.
- **The references page called `followup-emission` a journal emitter**; it emits follow-up tasks and verbose notes.
- **`followup-emission` §8's caller contract, and `session-hygiene`'s resume-pointer location, named `followup-emission` §4 for resolving the write target**, which lists what no longer produces follow-ups; the ladder is §2.
- **`phase-handoff`'s note on `/dev-workflows:implement`'s Phase 4.5** named every conformance note; only one in `$SPECS_PATH` reaches that phase.
- **`finding-triage`'s partly-emptied rule now admits a caller's own case**: a first-review `BLOCK` whose survivors a caller keeps from the fixer, because their fix lies in another code repository, none of them a `BLOCKER`, and that leaves no `BLOCKER` or `MAJOR` for it, asks the first settle prompt.
- **`next-phase-offer`'s routing graph and rule 5** name `/dev-workflows:implement`'s run from another repository, addressed to the unit it implemented (the Epic itself where it was one), before the remaining Epics, as the command's Next step does.

## [1.11.0] — 2026-10-03

### Added
- **`finding-triage` gains a third outcome, `unverified`.** A finding the orchestrator can neither confirm nor refute — the diff and the code around it leave the question open — is no longer dismissed as unsubstantiated: one that would be `MAJOR` or `BLOCKER` if true is recorded at that grade, marked `(unverified)`, with what would settle it, and reaches the user in the triage line; one that would be only `MINOR` or `NIT` is dismissed with the same note. It is never handed to a fixer and changes nothing the verdict gates. Prompted by BMAD's `maybe-false` triage verdict (3433612d, b0d27c3c).
- **`finding-triage` § On re-review.** Every re-review is now triaged too — any review over an artifact the run has already reviewed to a verdict, `/implement`'s review of its Phase 3.5 delta included. A finding at the same location — a code site or a document passage — making the same claim as a row this run already logged over the same artifact (for `/vuln` and `/upgrade`, the CVE's or component's own change), where the text there still reads as that row describes, keeps the row's outcome (`carried`): it is not verified again — except on a re-review the user chose to put the dispositions' reasons to the reviewer — and never handed to a fixer again; no survivor of a re-review is handed to a fixer; and the caller's stop acts on a review that **stayed blocked** — a `BLOCKER` surviving that triage, or a verdict the user keeps at a settle prompt — never on the verdict word. A second `BLOCK` with no surviving `BLOCKER` is the user's to settle, at a prompt with no re-review arm, never promoted silently. Prompted by BMAD 7c3e5827 and 85d968fc.
- **Triage raises a grade by effect, to `MAJOR` at most, and never lowers one**, and rules on each line of a `code-review` `### Declined to judge` list — a line ruled a defect is recorded, never handed to a fixer. Prompted by superpowers 5bf4e780 (#2319).
- **`escalation-rules`: `Review verdict BLOCK (unresolved after one fix cycle) — commands that fix inline`** — the heading five commands already cited (see Fixed).

### Changed
- **The patch gate never edits an instruction or contributor file the change did not itself edit** — `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.github/copilot-instructions.md`, a file under `.claude/rules/` or `.github/instructions/`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CODING_STANDARDS.md`: such a fix is surfaced for a human decision. `doc-fixer` applies the clause by the finding's location, since it is not handed the diff. `code-review` now reads `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `CODING_STANDARDS.md` and the `.claude/rules/` files that apply to the change as the repository's standards, which makes "edit the file to agree with the code" the likeliest wrong fix.
- **The triage line is one per review pass**, and survived + unverified + dismissed must sum to the findings reviewed. Where a settle prompt was asked, the line carries its answer, so a verdict the user settled is never left unmarked there; a re-review's line also counts the findings it carried and names every survivor, none of which is handed to a fixer.
- **The first settle prompt** — the one an emptied survivor set raises on a first review — now says every disposition is recorded and puts every disposition's reason to a re-review, and a caller that runs no re-review asks it without that arm. A reviewer with two grades (`BLOCKER` and `RECOMMENDATION`) maps onto the unverified and raise rules with `RECOMMENDATION` below `MAJOR`.
- **`model-routing/classification` §6**: a disposition may be an unverified record, and item 4 names the four edge-case checks `code-review` dimension 4 gained.

### Fixed
- **Five commands escalated per a `Review verdict BLOCK` rule `escalation-rules` did not have** (`/create-prd`, `/update-prd`, `/create-ard`, `/prd-proposal`, `/brd-proposal`) — only the `— /document` and `— /epics` variants existed — and `/design` cited the `— /epics` one, whose "Defer" appends a refinement note to the draft that `/design`'s own handoff then refuses. The new heading is the one all six cite (`product-workflows` 3.12.0, `dev-workflows` 4.6.0). `/specify` keeps the `— /epics` rule on purpose: it defines its own "Defer" to mirror it.

## [1.10.0] — 2026-10-02

### Added
- **`grilling-technique.md` gains the confirmation gate.** Reaching a shared understanding is the user's to declare, never the agent's to infer from a quiet turn: the gate fires wherever understanding is about to become an artifact or an irreversible step — before writing a section, and, for a caller that wrote section by section, once more over the whole artifact before the first reviewer dispatch, handoff or commit that follows. In autonomous or background invocation it cannot be self-satisfied and does not hold the write: the caller writes on its recorded open questions and its final report says the understanding was never confirmed. `/prompt-grill-me`'s own list of the mechanics names the gate. Ported from upstream `grilling`; the July harvest had skipped it as "already a superset", which covered only the autonomous half. Previously the Mechanics ended *"Continue until you and the user reach a shared understanding … then write that section"*, which left the judgement with the agent.
- **At the gate, the agent plays its understanding back** — the intended outcome, the constraints and what success looks like, marking what the user said and what was inferred — and asks for confirmation or correction. The play-back asks no decision, so it spends no slot of a bounded caller's cap. Adapted from superpowers' brainstorming skill. `docs/reference/references.md` describes the gate.

### Changed
- **§4's commit scan takes no merge commit** (`--no-merges`). A merge commit's range is the whole branch it merged — work the record and the scan already reach commit by commit — or, for the base merged into a work branch, the base's own work; and a forge writes a merged pull request's `[<key>]` title into the merge commit, so nearly every merge-commit landing came back as unrecorded work. The cost, stated in §4: work a merge commit's message carries alone is not found. The unanchored branch-name probe keeps merge commits, so on a repository the scan left at zero matches that is where such a commit shows, labelled *"may name this key inside a branch name or in a merge commit"* — elsewhere it shows nowhere; the run's scanned count is the non-merge commits walked, and the boundary paragraph says what a wider boundary would add and why it is not taken here.

### Fixed
- **`implementation-format.md` §1 said a merged branch's recorded commit "stays reachable from the base"** for every merge style. After a squash or a rebase merge no branch reaches it once its own is deleted; the paragraph now says what is true per merge style, and that how a landed commit is read is `diff-summarizer`'s rule. The example entry's `# squashed` annotation, left over from when the operator wrote the commit, is gone.
- **`grilling-technique.md`'s relentless depth said "keep walking the tree"**, a mechanic the file had replaced with asking from the frontier.

## [1.9.1] — 2026-10-02

### Changed

- **`defect-reporter` runs on the §2.1 Sonnet chain, the tier of the `impl-maintenance` it replaces, instead of a cheap chain headed by Haiku.** Under `--skip-feedback` it is the run's only post-session feedback capture (`emit-block` still fires on a halt), and its work is judgement: it applies the defect predicate and its exclusions, then tries to confirm the wrong line in the plugin's source. Haiku saved cents on one dispatch per run. `classification.md` §2.2 is retired, its `defect-reporter` rule now sits in §2.1, and `defect_model` records a §2.1 resolution. `run-flags` §4, `/frames`, `defect-reporter`'s description and the docs pages say the same.
- **`run-flags` §2 now carries the Haiku rows itself.** The `haiku` alias, and a version-specific Haiku value in a family-only harness, used to resolve against §2.2; no `classification.md` chain names Haiku any longer, so `--enforce-model=haiku` and `haiku4.5` resolve exactly as before, against `run-flags` §2's own rows.

### Fixed

- **`cost-emission.md` said Haiku was reached only by §2.2 and by an enforced model**, which omitted a session running on Haiku and left Fable out of the priced models. It now names Haiku and Fable, with Opus 4.5 and Mythos, which the table also keys, as models no chain names but a session or an enforced model can run on. `cost-prices.yaml`'s comment listed the Sonnet chain without Sonnet 5.5, and now lists all four rows.
- **`classification.md` §4's example `model_routing` block named `claude-sonnet-5`** for `detection_model` and `implementation_model`; it now names `claude-sonnet-5-5`, the chain's first row.

## [1.9.0] — 2026-10-02

### Changed

- **`grounding-format` §8: independence is structural, not a discipline the verifier keeps (#73).** The step that re-derives is handed the requirement premise and the source, never the finding's verdict, evidence, control or citation, and reads neither the specs repository's files nor its history, which can name them; verification is a blind re-derivation followed by a comparison, each its own dispatch. §4.1 says the baseline re-derivation is the blind step's integrity re-run. §2.2 now says the verifier is handed `control` only in its compare step. `product-workflows` 3.11.0 implements it.
- **§8 now says a disputed `contradict` gets a second blind re-derivation.** The comparison never revises a blind result, but it flags one whose own evidence does not establish its own verdict. Where a `contradict` rests on such a verdict, and not on the control route, a second blind derivation decides: agreement upholds the `contradict`, and disagreement makes the outcome `unprovable`, with notes recording the disagreement. A control-forced `contradict` is never disputed, even where the blind verdict also differs, since that route is deterministic and takes precedence. §2.1 lists `blind_disputed` among the verifier's return fields, never a record field.

### Fixed

- **`grounding-format` did not say whether a baseline finding owes a positive control**, and a live verifier ruled that it did, forcing `contradict` on every run's baseline; §2.2 and §4.1 now say it owes none, because `baseline-integrity`'s three commands are git's own report over the whole checkout rather than a search, and §8's `not-owed` row names it.
- **§4.1 now says a run that mints a baseline finding supersedes that repository's earlier baseline findings**, each one an earlier `baselines.md` entry records by id, so a plain re-run no longer leaves two live baseline findings for one repository and pin; an entry that records no id, or an id no block carries, supersedes nothing and is reported.
- **§4 never said what names the repository in a `baselines.md` entry**; step 1 now fixes it as the clone's remote slug, the identifier `/prd-ground` Phase 1 resolves a repository by, so every reader that compares entries compares slugs rather than paths or directory names.
- **`phase-handoff` §2.4 named a fixed model, `Claude Opus 5`, in the deliverable commit's `Co-Authored-By` trailer**, which goes stale with every model release; the trailer now names the model the session runs on.

## [1.8.5] — 2026-10-02

### Fixed

- **A `$SPECS_PATH` pointing at `specifications/` itself, at a mount of it, or at a folder inside it resolved every key `absent`, and a command that creates the folder it did not find then created `specifications/specifications/<KIND>-<KEY>-<slug>/`** (#70, behaviour). `addressing.md` §3 gains the `specs-root-check` entry point, which `resolve-address` runs as its step 0 wherever `$SPECS_PATH` is set, **on an `@<path>` as well as a `<KEY>`**. An `@<path>` run would otherwise meet the same stop at a later lookup by key, after its expensive work, or have it read as `none`. It tests two signals on the filesystem:
  - **(a) `$SPECS_PATH` is itself the `specifications/` directory.** One test is `git -C "$SPECS_PATH" rev-parse --show-prefix` printing `specifications/` or a prefix beginning with it, which catches a folder of any name inside the tree, a legacy unprefixed one included. **That test runs whether or not `$SPECS_PATH/specifications/` exists**, so a tree an earlier run already damaged, by creating its folders at `specifications/specifications/`, is caught too. The other test, which also runs whether or not a nested `specifications/` exists, is `[ "$SPECS_PATH/../specifications" -ef "$SPECS_PATH" ]` where `$SPECS_PATH` is not itself a work tree's top level. A specs repository cloned into a directory named `specifications` is never taken for one, and a `specifications/` mount a run already wrote into is caught.
  - **(b) `$SPECS_PATH` is, or directly holds, a specs folder**, tested whether or not a `specifications/` exists there. A carrier counts only where its directory's name passes §4.1's prefix test. `$SPECS_PATH`'s own name is read physically (`cd -P`, so a symlink is judged by what it reaches), and a subfolder whose physical path lies inside `$SPECS_PATH/specifications/` (a convenience link such as `current`) is skipped, so a correct root holding one never stops. The prefix is `<KIND>-<key>-`, with the key read from the carrier. So a root's `templates/`, `README.md` or `docs/` carrying `key:` and a folder `kind:` never stops it, fresh or populated. **The blind spot, stated as a class:** a `$SPECS_PATH` outside any repository's top-level `specifications/` that is, or holds only, folders not named `<KIND>-<key>-`, such as a mount of a legacy unprefixed folder or of a PRD folder under another name. Either `$SPECS_PATH` carries a §4 carrier of its own, or an immediate subdirectory outside §2's reserved names carries one. That catches the damage on a PRD folder an earlier `/idea` wrote `specifications/` into, and on a `specifications/` directory mounted under another name after any run wrote into it. Reserved names such as `revisions/` and `brd/` are skipped because they hold copies asserting their parent's key.

  Either signal is a hard stop, `status: misrooted`, printed as `SPECS_PATH_INSIDE_TREE`. The value it names comes from a walk up with `cd -P`, which accepts a directory only where it and its parent lie in the same git work tree. So a symlinked `$SPECS_PATH` resolves to the real repository, and a `specifications/` directory mounted at a non-repository parent is never sent to that parent, which would draw the preflight's "not a repository" notice next. The stop writes the walk's result as `<the value the walk found>` and keeps `<value>` for `$SPECS_PATH` alone, so an agent cannot attach the next clause to the wrong path. Where the value the walk found is not its repository's top level, the stop names it but says the layout is unsupported. Where no value can be established, the stop says *the directory that holds `specifications/`*. The stop is one line. Where a misrooted run left anything behind, it adds one more line naming it and where it belongs: a nested `specifications/` and its folders, and any of `dev-workflows-feedback/`, `dev-workflows-cost/` and `documentation/<slug>/dev-workflows/` under `$SPECS_PATH`. `dev-workflows-feedback/` and `dev-workflows-cost/` there were never staged, so G1 fired on every run. A `documentation/<slug>/dev-workflows/` there, and the bookkeeping and any `implementation.md` in a nested `specifications/`, are never committed while the variable stays wrong: seen from the repository's top level their paths begin `specifications/`, so §2.1's `^(specs|specifications|vis)/.+/dev-workflows/` shape or its single-file shape takes them for artifacts, but `git add` from `$SPECS_PATH` names a path that does not exist and fails. The first run after the variable is fixed would commit them in the wrong place unless they are moved first, and would resolve a key into a nested tree left in place; the line says so for each. Where only a stray specs folder fired and `$SPECS_PATH` is a repository's top level, or a directory in a repository holding `specifications/`, the stop takes a stray-folder form: the variable names the right directory, every stray folder is named with `specifications/` as where it belongs, the unsupported-layout clause is added below a repository's top level, and no leftover line is added. Where nothing on disk tells a specs root from a specs tree under another name (no repository, or below the top level with no `specifications/`), the stop states both readings and what each needs; the first names the `specifications/` the folders belong in and sets `SPECS_PATH` to its parent, never to a directory that holds no `specifications/`. The line ends "move them yourself, commit the move, then re-run — the plugin moves nothing". **It gives no commands**: a move has to be right in every state the repository might be in, and three review rounds found scripted ones that were not. A specs repository nothing has been written into yet matches neither signal. The stop offers no "enter the path" option, since the wrong value lives in the environment. **Two layouts are unsupported, each for its own reason.** A specs tree inside a larger repository is unsupported because of git (below). A specs root that is itself a subdirectory named `specifications` is unsupported because of detection: it cannot be told from the damaged tree, so it stops on every run until it is moved to the ordinary layout.
- **A stopped or noticed run still wrote its bookkeeping under the misrooted path** (#70). For example, `/create-prd` with `SPECS_PATH=R/specifications` wrote a pending cost to `R/specifications/dev-workflows-cost/`, which G1 then flagged on every run after the fix, and which never reconciled. The stop and both §3.1 notices now set **`specs_git: misrooted`** for the whole run, as G0 sets `specs_git: blocked`. Under it:
  - cost (§8), feedback (§2), follow-ups (§2, with a notice naming the flag) and `resume.md` (`session-hygiene` §1) fall to report-only;
  - the preflight runs no guard and performs no write: no leftover flush, push retry, branch switch, pull, commit or push (a flush there failed `git add` with exit 128 on every run, or committed at a correct root holding a stray folder). Its fetch and its branch classification still run, so `require-on-main` reads a fresh `<default-ref>` and row B still passes a resumed `/design` on its own branch; a row that would switch stays and prints one line naming the branch;
  - `commit-artifacts` skips with the notice repeated and its own outcome line;
  - `phase-handoff` §2.1's `handoff-to-main` refuses the handoff, never silently, and §4.3 prints one line above the unchanged consent array, on this flag or on `specs_git: blocked`, saying the first option will be refused;
  - `require-on-main`'s row C offers no branch-switching repair.

  So a stopped run writes nothing under the path, and a noticed run that goes on (`/create-prd` on `M/specs`) writes what the command itself writes (its deliverable, and `implementation.md` or a draft) where the command puts it and leaves it uncommitted. None of `specs-preflight`, `commit-artifacts`, `handoff-to-main` and `require-on-main` switches a branch or commits in that repository; a command that changes code or documentation still branches in the repository it changed, which in that layout holds the specs tree, one more reason the layout is unsupported. The stop says exactly that it wrote nothing under the path, replacing "nothing was created". `implementation.md`, `/document`'s drafts and `/release-notes`' draft are named as written and not committed under either flag, and the documentation commands' landing sentences name the report-only exception. Every command's preflight and terminal step names both flags, and `.claude/rules/workflows-core-git.md` no longer calls detached HEAD the only state in which `commit-artifacts` skips. `specs-repo-git` §3.7 calls G0 the one *guard* that refuses to commit, and no longer says the flag stops every commit. `/frames` says a run carrying the flag runs no guard, so a declined index handoff draws no G1 there.
- **`commit-artifacts` had two outcomes for one state** (#70). A `$SPECS_PATH` that is not a repository but where `specs-root-check` finds a signal, such as `SPECS_PATH=/workspace/specs` bind-mounting `R/specifications` on an `/upgrade` run, fails the environment gate and carries `specs_git: misrooted`. Step 1 said both "silent no-op" and "re-emit the notice", and §6 had a row for each. The flags are now tested first, and the misrooted outcome and its §6 row take precedence; the silent row applies only to a run carrying neither flag. §3.1 no longer says nothing later repeats a no-address run's notice: `commit-artifacts` repeats it verbatim.
- **`require-on-main` read a bare `<ref>:<path>`**, which git resolves from the repository's top level, while its `diff` pathspec resolves from `$SPECS_PATH` (#70). In a specs tree inside a larger repository, which the preflight notices and lets run on, a run resumed on its own branch fell to row F, and a path written from the top level made `diff` match nothing, so every divergence passed as row A. `phase-handoff` §3.2 now takes the path relative to `$SPECS_PATH` and writes every object name `<ref>:./<path>`, as `/brd-interview`'s anchor read already did. In the supported layout both forms name the same object.
- **`ard-resolution.md` mapped only `absent` to `none`, so a `misrooted` status had no stated outcome** (#70). It now names `misrooted` as a stop, never `none`, and `/frames` Phase 0's status list names it too.
- **`specs-preflight`'s notice for a `$SPECS_PATH` that is not a git repository now names the cause when the path is a `specifications/` directory mounted on its own** (#70). `rev-parse` fails there because the git directory sits above the mount point. Where `specs-root-check`'s signals hold, the notice says which one and what to set, so a command that resolves no address learns the cause too.
- **On an ordinary checkout, a `$SPECS_PATH` set to `<repo>/specifications` passed `specs-preflight`'s gate silently**, because git finds the repository above it (#70). A run that resolves no address, such as `/upgrade`, `/docs-audit` or a logging command, then filed its bookkeeping inside `specifications/`. Where the gate passes, §3.1 now runs `specs-root-check`'s tests, preconditions included. Where a signal holds, it prints a notice naming the variable, its value, the signal and the value to set, and with the stop's own text: one line, the unsupported-layout clause where it applies, and the second line where a misrooted run left something behind. The run continues. §3.1 also says which callers print it before their stop: `/idea`, and `/vuln` on a token carrying a key, run the preflight before they resolve. A `$SPECS_PATH` like `<repo>/specs` holding `specs/specifications/` matches no signal, and draws the below-top-level notice instead. §1 rule 7 and §3's opening no longer say `specs-preflight` speaks only when it acts or a guard fires, and §3 lists `misrooted` among the address stops that run no preflight.
- **A specs tree inside a larger repository is now named as unsupported** (#70). This plugin switches branches and commits in the repository `$SPECS_PATH` belongs to, so in one that also holds code it moves that checkout. Where `specs-preflight`'s gate passes and `git -C "$SPECS_PATH" rev-parse --show-prefix` is non-empty, §3.1 prints one notice naming the variable, its value and the repository's top level, then continues. It is skipped where the inside-tree notice already fired. `specs-repo-git` §3.1 and the four `environment.md` pages state the rule. The staging classifier, anchored at the repository root, is correct for the dedicated repository that is now the only supported layout.
- **The preflight step said it was "Prompt-free and silent when the specs repo is clean and on its default branch"**, which a misconfigured `$SPECS_PATH` now falsifies (#70). Every such sentence takes `specs-repo-git` §1 rule 7's wording instead: silent unless it acts, a guard fires, or §3.1 reports a misconfigured `$SPECS_PATH`. This applies to `/frames` and the four logging commands.

### Fixed — documentation

- `docs/reference/environment.md` gains *When it points inside the specs tree*. Its *unreadable or unwritable* paragraph no longer says every such state is reported: a read-only specs mount degrades silently, and only a path that is not a directory or not a repository is reported. `docs/getting-started.md` says to set `SPECS_PATH` to the root of a dedicated specs repository, the directory that holds `specifications/`. The `/feedback` and `/frames` pages no longer say the preflight is silent on every clean repository on its default branch. Every page, the `README.md` included, states where `$SPECS_PATH` points in those same words. `feedback-emission.md` places an unfiled entry at the top of `$SPECS_PATH` rather than at "the specs-repo root". `/frames` Phase 4 lists `SPECS_PATH_INSIDE_TREE` among Phase 0's stops.
- `docs/reference/environment.md` no longer says nothing is switched to another branch under `specs_git: misrooted`. The specs-repository steps commit nothing and switch no branch, the preflight still fetches and names any branch it stayed on, and a command that changes code or documentation still branches in the repository it changed.
- The session-cost and session-feedback pages defined `specs_git: misrooted` as a `$SPECS_PATH` set inside the specs tree or below its repository's top level, which leaves out a correct root holding a stray specs folder. They now point at the environment page and cite `workflows-core:specs-repo-git` §3.1 instead of restating the cases. The environment page says how the stop reads where `$SPECS_PATH` holds a stray folder below a repository's top level or is no repository, and that a nested `specifications/` left in place is committed from, and searched for keys, once the variable is fixed.

## [1.8.4] — 2026-10-01

### Fixed — the repository's gates (not shipped in any plugin)

- **`scripts/validate-catalog.py` counted a rules file's own path as a live `paths:` match.** A rules file is a real file, so a glob naming only itself — or two rules naming only each other — passed, though such a rule loads only when a rules file is itself opened, never during the work it governs. A match under `.claude/rules/` no longer counts, and the error says why. `--selftest` 26 → 31: the self-reference, the mutual pair, and a pair sharing one `**/*.md` glob that passes only while a file outside the folder also matches, each confirmed to fail against a gate that excludes only the file itself and one that rejects any glob touching the folder; plus a case for the existing worktree exclusion inside the `paths:` loop, which nothing exercised.
- **`scripts/check-docs.sh` check 11 read a handoff's declared paths from the start of the `deliverable_paths` line to the end of the `title:` line.** A line routinely cites a reference file before the token or names the deliverable again after `title:` — `/dev-workflows:design`'s does the latter, and `/dev-workflows:implement`'s and `/product-workflows:idea`'s the former — so a declaration reworded to name no path still looked extractable. None of those three is examined by the check today, so nothing was missed yet. The span now starts at the token and ends at `title:`; two selftest cases, one per bound, each confirmed red first.
- **`scripts/check-id-grammar.sh` walked `scripts/mermaid/node_modules/`**, the git-ignored tree `npm ci` installs the mermaid gate's parser into, so a local run after the install scanned every third-party markdown file there. CI was unaffected, because the install runs after that gate. The anchored exclusion list now names it, and a selftest case pins it.

## [1.8.3] — 2026-10-01

### Fixed — behaviour

- **`docs-grounding` again tells an index nobody can reach from an index nobody built — narrowly.** 1.8.2 rightly stopped inferring registry corruption from a positive global `Vectors:` count, which can belong to other collections, but it also put every uncovered root back on the build prompt, including the state the rule was first written for: `qmd collection list` reporting **no collections at all** while `qmd status` reports indexed documents. No other collection can own those documents, so that state is visible from the two probes alone. Step 3.5 now handles it as its own case: the no-collection prompt is still shown, but *Skip* is recommended, the build option says it may re-embed documents the index already holds, and the plan-approval line names the document count and points at `qmd status`. A collection that exists and does not cover the root is unchanged. `docs-grounder`'s rung 3 records the same distinction in `notes`. The cause is still not diagnosed and nothing is repaired.
- **A fast-mode Opus 4.6 turn was priced as unknown.** Opus 4.6 accepts `speed: "fast"` and runs and bills it at its standard rate, as the pricing page and this table's own header say; with no `fast:` block the engine reported `unpriced-speed:fast` and a null cost. Its block now repeats the standard rates. Opus 4.7, which rejects fast mode, still has none.
- **Claude Opus 4.5 had no price key.** It is outside the §2 chain, but a session can run on it by hand, and no other key prefixes `claude-opus-4-5`, so such a run priced `unpriced-model`. Keyed at $5 / $25.
- **`session-cost.py --selftest` left a temporary directory behind on every run.** The body returns early on several failures and removed nothing on any path; it now runs inside a wrapper that removes its tree whichever way it returns.

Both pricing fixes carry shipped-table selftest pins, each confirmed to fail with its key removed. `cost-emission.md` §4 states the Opus 4.6 / 4.7 split. `hooks/test-notify.sh`'s Maven comment now warns that a hand-written fixture must keep Surefire's per-class suffix, without which a per-class line is counted again.

### Changed — documentation

- **`docs/family-map.md`** gains a legend entry and a gray node for the manual backlog work after `/docs-audit`, qualifies the command names a built-in shadows, draws `/prd-ground` as two nodes (BRD slice, idea PRD), and draws the hand-overs `/brd-interview` and `/update-prd` actually make.

## [1.8.2] — 2026-09-30

**Update `dev-workflows` to 4.4.0, `docs-workflows` to 1.4.2 and `product-workflows` to 3.10.0 together with this release**: their chain-head references now name `claude-sonnet-5-5`, which this release adds to the §2 and §2.1 chains.

### Fixed — behaviour

- **`docs-grounding` distinguishes missing root coverage from failed probes without inferring registry corruption.** Global qmd vectors can belong entirely to unrelated collections. When successful user-scope probes find no collection covering the docs root, the consented build choice remains available regardless of that count; failed probes and a shadowing project-local index still fall back without building. The plan-approval line reports missing root coverage, not a nonexistent index or an unsupported repair diagnosis.
- **The `qmd-vector` rung was selected on a boolean test over a proportional fact.** A partly embedded collection passes "has embeddings", so the plan-approval line reported semantic retrieval while `qmd vsearch` returned noise for most queries. Partial coverage keeps the rung and appends a clause naming both counts.
- **`docs-grounder`'s fallback names the observed precondition.** Its notes distinguish an absent binary, a failed or timed-out probe, a shadowing project-local index, and no collection covering the docs root. It shares the resolver's root-coverage test and does not turn unrelated global vectors into a registry-corruption claim.
- **A Skill-tool invocation never cut the cost window.** The `<command-name>` envelope is emitted only when the user *types* the slash command; a prose request reaching the command through the **Skill tool** appears solely as an assistant `tool_use` block. `command_envelope` read user messages only, so a prose-invoked run cut nothing and §13.3's "segment to the next boundary of any kind" ran straight through it — measured on a real transcript as a typed grill command absorbing two later prose-invoked runs. Both shapes are now read. **The Skill half resolves against the manifest where the typed half deliberately does not**: the typed half is permissive because a user can only type a real command, while commands dispatch non-command skills constantly (`model-routing`, the `reference` loader, several times a run) and cutting on those would shatter one window into spurious segments. Seven selftest cases pin both halves so neither can be "harmonised" into the other.
- **Fable 5.1 cache reads were priced 4x high.** `cost-prices.yaml` keyed only `claude-fable-5` and its comment asserted the longest-prefix rule covered 5.1 from that entry — true of `input`/`output`, false of `cache_read`, the field that dominates a cached agentic session: Fable 5.1 bills cache reads at 0.025x input ($0.25), a per-model exception, not the 0.1x ($1.00) every other model uses. Both ids are now keyed separately and the false claim is replaced by a statement of why it was false.
- **`claude-sonnet-5-5` was unreachable and unpriced.** §2.1 still headed with `claude-sonnet-5`, so no routing path could select the current Sonnet, and pricing was correct only by the coincidence that `claude-sonnet-5` is a strict prefix of it with currently-equal rates — the same latent shape that priced Opus 5.5 about 25 percent high until 1.7.5. Added at the head of §2.1 and as §2's first Sonnet fallback, with the 41 literal chain-head references across the commands, both `model-routing.md` docs pages, their "family's newest model" claim, and `run-flags.md`'s unpriced-model check all moved in the same change.
- **The selftest proved the engine and never the shipped table**, which is how the Fable gap survived. Shipped-table pins added for all six keyed ids. The Sonnet pin is a **key-presence** assertion rather than a price one: 5.5 and 5 bill identically today, so dropping the key leaves every price check green. Both mutation-verified.
- **Fast mode and US-only inference were recorded by the transcript and ignored by the engine.** Every assistant message carries `usage.speed` and `usage.inference_geo`; `session-cost.py` read neither, and the price table said those modifiers were "NOT applied (interactive Claude Code sessions bill at standard rates)" — false from the first `/fast` session, and wrong in one direction only: a fast Opus 5.5 turn bills $8/$40 against the $4/$20 keyed, 50 percent low, and `inference_geo: us` 10 percent low. The engine now buckets each model's tokens by `(speed, geo)` and prices each bucket at its own rate. **Fast mode is an explicit per-model `fast:` sub-block**, never a multiplier, in keeping with the table's rule that every rate is keyed — and it has to be: prompt-caching multipliers apply on top of the *fast* input price, so Opus 5.5's fast cache read is 0.05 × $8, not 0.05 × $4. Keyed on Opus 5.5, Opus 5 and Opus 4.8, the three models it exists on. **Data residency is `modifiers.inference_geo.us: 1.1`**, applied to every category and stacking on fast mode.
- **An unknown is null, never the standard rate.** A `fast` record on a model with no `fast:` block is `unpriced-speed:fast`; a residency with no multiplier is `unpriced-inference-geo:<value>`; both report a null cost. §6.1's dominance warning now fires on any null-cost row and names which of the three reasons applies, since two of them mean the table is *behind* rather than the model *missing*. A row priced at a non-default rate carries `modifiers: [speed:fast]` (or `inference-geo:us`, or both), so a cost per token that looks high explains itself; its token counts stay the plain totals.
- **Backward compatible by construction.** `global`, `not_available` and an absent field are the standard case and are never looked up, so a `$DEV_WORKFLOWS_COST_PRICES` override written before the `modifiers` map existed prices every global record exactly as it did. 21 new selftest cases, built through `add_usage` rather than by hand; mutation-verified.
- **`claude-mythos-5-1` and `claude-mythos-5` are keyed**, beside the Fable pair they price identically to and with the same trap: `claude-mythos-5` is a strict prefix of `claude-mythos-5-1`, and 5.1's cache reads are 0.025× input where 5's are 0.1×.
- **`hooks/test-notify.sh`'s gradle branch dropped a failing subproject.** `first()` returns `m[-1]`, so a multi-project build reported only the last subproject's numbers and a red build could notify as green. Now sums across subprojects, reading each line's failed count from that line's own trailing clauses so a stray `, N failed` elsewhere in the log is not summed in. Verified: `13 completed, 3 failed` where the old code said `5 completed, 3 failed`; single-project and green-run behaviour unchanged.

## [1.8.1] — 2026-09-29

**Update `dev-workflows` to 4.3.1, `docs-workflows` to 1.4.1 and `product-workflows` to 3.9.1 together with this release**: their nested-dispatch agents and model-routing pages now follow the §5 dispatch rule below.

### Fixed — behaviour

- **`--enforce-model` and every model-routing dispatch assumed the agent tool accepts full model ids; Claude Code's accepts only family names.** The Agent tool's `model` parameter enumerates `sonnet | opus | haiku | fable` and nothing else, so `run-flags` §2 read reachability from a list in which no resolved id such as `claude-opus-5-5` ever appears — an explicit `--enforce-model=opus5.5` either stopped wrongly with `RUN_FLAGS_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` or left the agent to guess — and any dispatch passing a full id as `model:` (`classification.md` §5's own example among them) failed the tool's schema unless the orchestrator translated it on its own initiative. Now:
  - `classification.md` §2 defines a **family-only harness**: where the parameter enumerates only family names, a chain row counts as available when its family is listed, and the harness decides which version of the family runs.
  - `classification.md` §5 states the **dispatch rule** for every dispatch in every command and agent: the `model_routing` record keeps the resolved id as the record of intent, while `model:` passes what the tool accepts — the id where it accepts ids, otherwise its family name (`claude-opus-*` → `opus`, `claude-sonnet-*` → `sonnet`, `claude-haiku-*` → `haiku`, `claude-fable-*` → `fable`). §10's dispatch and nested-dispatch bullets follow it, and so do `upgrade-executor`'s, `vuln-fixer`'s and `docs-style-checker`'s own nested dispatches.
  - `run-flags` §2: in a family-only harness a bare family alias is reachable iff listed and is recorded as the family (`enforced_model: opus`); a version-specific form (`opus5.5`, `claude-opus-5-5`) is honoured only when it is its family's newest chain row, and is then passed as the family name; any older one (`opus5`) stops `RUN_FLAGS_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` with a message saying this harness selects models by family only and suggesting `--enforce-model=<family>`. `fable` is now an alias row of its own (where the parameter accepts ids, the reachable `claude-fable-…` id highest by version number, stopping `RUN_FLAGS_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` naming `fable` when none is reachable). Where the parameter accepts ids, behaviour is unchanged, except that bare `fable` is now accepted. The `run_flags` record gains `requested` (the value as written), and the `Run flags:` line renders the enforced value exactly as the `Model routing:` line does. The unpriced-model check prices a bare family by its newest chain row, and §3 step 7's relaunch advisory compares by family there.
  - The `Model routing: bypassed — enforced <id> (flag|env)` line (`run-flags` §6, `classification.md` §10) now renders `<id>` as what the dispatches were actually passed, plus the requested value where it differs — `enforced opus (from opus5.5; the harness picks the version) (flag)`.
  - The environment reference, the getting-started page, and `dev-workflows`' and `product-workflows`' model-routing pages say what a family-only harness does with a version-specific value.

## [1.8.0] — 2026-09-29

**Update `dev-workflows`, `product-workflows` and `docs-workflows` together with this release**: their commands now cite the new `run-flags` reference and dispatch the new `defect-reporter` agent.

### Added

- **Three run flags, every applicable command's own body deciding which apply to it: `--skip-costs`, `--skip-feedback`, `--enforce-model=<model>`**, plus their `$WORKFLOWS_SKIP_COSTS` / `$WORKFLOWS_SKIP_FEEDBACK` / `$WORKFLOWS_ENFORCE_MODEL` environment defaults (a flag beats its environment variable, which beats off), documented start to finish in the new `workflows-core:run-flags` reference and resolved by its `strip-run-flags` entry point. A flag may sit anywhere in a command's argument list — before, after, or interleaved with its own flags and address — except inside the prose span of a free-text command (today `/feedback`, `/prompt`, `/prompt-brainstorm`, `/prompt-grill-me`, `/implement`, `/idea`, `/brd-split`, and `/document` in direct mode), where only the leading and the trailing run of flag tokens is stripped and everything between is kept verbatim — prose that genuinely ends in a flag name loses it to that trailing strip, documented on each affected command's own page.
- **`--skip-costs` keeps attribution correct while writing nothing.** The cost phase runs `run-flags`' new `skip-cost` entry point in place of `cost-emission`'s `emit-cost`: the chained checkpoint still advances, so the next command's window starts in the right place, and any record a ceded `/prompt-brainstorm` or `/prompt-grill-me` run deferred for later replay is dropped instead of replayed, reported once per record dropped.
- **`--skip-feedback` swaps the maintenance dispatch for a new bugs-only agent, `defect-reporter`.** In place of `impl-maintenance`'s Lessons Learned report, the run dispatches `defect-reporter` (Read/Glob/Grep, no frontmatter `model:` pin — the new §2.2 cheap Haiku-first chain, or the run's enforced model) and persists only what it returns as real defects — location, evidence, minimal repro — through `feedback-emission`'s new `emit-bugs` entry point; a run with no defects persists nothing. The in-session Lessons Learned report is the one thing this flag costs.
- **A defect in the `ihudak/ai-containers` environment is now a feedback category, `environment-defect`, on every run, not only a bugs-only one.** `feedback-emission`'s plugin-facing predicate widened to include it; `emit-auto` now also projects a Key observation naming one as `category: environment-defect`; and `emit-block` (capture-at-block) now also fires on a halt over a tool the ai-containers image is meant to provide and lacks, alongside its existing plugin-gap predicate — never for a tool missing on the user's own machine or from any container not built from ai-containers.
- **`--enforce-model=<model>` pins every subagent dispatch of a run to one model**, bypassing model routing's own per-step chain selection (new `classification.md` §10): every dispatch passes `model: <enforced>` explicitly, overriding even a frontmatter `model: opus` pin, and a nested dispatch (`docs-style-checker` → `prose-style-checker`; `upgrade-executor` / `vuln-fixer` → `test-baseliner`) propagates it to its own `task` calls. The orchestrator itself always stays on its own session model — enforcement pins subagent dispatches, never the session — and prints one relaunch advisory when the two differ. Every gate or degrade path that tests `current_model` to require or prefer an Opus session (`/design`'s and `/create-ard`'s HARD gates, `/prd-proposal`'s and `/brd-proposal`'s stops, `/create-prd`'s degrade path, among others) does not fire under enforcement, since the user has already chosen the model.
- New agent `workflows-core:defect-reporter` and new reference `workflows-core:run-flags`.
- `/frames`' own docs page now says a pending deferred cost record from `/prompt-brainstorm` or `/prompt-grill-me` is dropped under `--skip-costs`, matching `/feedback`'s and `/prompt`'s pages, which already said so.

## [1.7.8] — 2026-09-26

**Update `product-workflows` to 3.8.5 with this release**, whose `/idea` Phase 3 applies the rule below to the `stated_scope` its `idea-reader` now returns.

### Fixed — behaviour

- **`grilling-technique` had no rule for a decision the source had already made.** The fact-vs-decision split told a grill what to look up and what to ask, but said nothing about an explicit inclusion, exclusion or constraint the ingested material (or an earlier answer) already stated. A grill could therefore re-ask it, or, worse, put a recommendation whose rationale assumed the opposite. A new mechanic makes such a statement settled: it is not re-asked, no question, recommended answer or **rationale** may assume the opposite, and revisiting it takes an explicit challenge that quotes the source. A hedged statement is put to the user to confirm, recommending the source's position. It applies to every caller of this file.

### Fixed — the repository's gates (not shipped in any plugin)

- **`scripts/check-docs.sh` check 6 counted bytes, not characters, under `mawk`**, the default `awk` on Debian and Ubuntu containers. A 199-character table cell carrying a few arrows or em dashes read as over 200, so `main` failed 15 times locally while passing on a runner whose `awk` is `gawk`. The check now runs under `LC_ALL=C` and subtracts UTF-8 continuation bytes, so both `awk`s count characters; it assumes well-formed UTF-8, which the comment says. A new selftest case, a 190-character cell of multibyte characters, fails on the old code.
- **Check 18 was inert in a C or POSIX locale.** Its `[—-]` bracket expression is a set of bytes there, so the three-byte em dash never matched, and an `— Unreleased` heading on a publishing ref passed; the selftest's red case failed locally with `LANG` unset. The pattern is now the alternation `(—|-)`, and the selftest passes under both `LC_ALL=C` and `C.UTF-8`.

## [1.7.7] — 2026-09-24

### Changed — the marketplace is now `shipwright`, and the repository `ihudak/ai-workflows`

The marketplace was `ihudak-plugins`, at `ihudak/ihudak-claude-plugins`. GitHub redirects the old repository URL, but the marketplace name is part of every install key (`<plugin>@ihudak-plugins`), so moving to the new name means registering the marketplace again. Removing a marketplace uninstalls the plugins installed from it, so reinstall each one you had:

```bash
claude plugin marketplace remove ihudak-plugins
claude plugin marketplace add ihudak/ai-workflows
claude plugin install <plugin>@shipwright
```

Run the last line once per plugin you use, then restart Claude Code. Environment variables and your specs, docs and code repositories are not touched. This plugin's `homepage` and `repository` now point at the new repository.

**Update `product-workflows` with this release** — to 3.8.4. `next-phase-offer`'s BRD-route list now names the hand-over `/brd-interview` offers on a slice that needs no customer review, and 3.8.4 is the `product-workflows` release whose `/brd-interview` offers it.

### Added

- **A family map** (`docs/family-map.md`): every slash command of `product-workflows`, `dev-workflows`, `docs-workflows` and `workflows-core` on one diagram, in role lanes, coloured by plugin, with the deliverable each command hands the next on the arrows. The repository README links to it.

### Changed

- `next-phase-offer`'s `/brd-interview` entry names the hand-over into `/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` on a slice with nothing left for the customer to review, and its `/brd-reconcile` entry says it is the hand-over for a slice the customer reviewed. The rule that the station offering the three must make the advance/re-entry split no longer names `/brd-reconcile` as the only such station.
- The Workflow diagram's `/frames` edge names its reader — design grounding in `/product-workflows:prd-ground` — and its nodes use the family map's colours.

## [1.7.6] — 2026-09-23

**Update `dev-workflows`, `product-workflows` and `docs-workflows` with this release** — to 4.2.4, 3.8.3 and 1.3.4. Its `grounding-format`, `phase-handoff`, `escalation-rules` and `next-phase-offer` now describe stops and behaviour those releases carry, and beside an older one they are live instructions the command files contradict.

### Changed
- `addressing.md` §1 no longer says the repo-root `CLAUDE.md` names the defect family a locally re-stated key grammar belongs to. That account moved to `docs/maintainers/rationale.md`, section `resolve-against-a-known-set`, and §1 now points there — to *the plugin source repository's* copy, since at run time "this repository" would mean the user's, which carries no such file. §4's citation of the standing rule now names it, *Resolve an identifier against a known set*, which is still in `CLAUDE.md`. Wording only, no behaviour. (The run-time reading of §1's pointer was corrected before this version was released, so it is folded into this entry rather than bumped again.)

### Fixed — behaviour
- **A verifier's `contradict` on an on-file finding — one written before the verifying run began — no longer rewrites it in place.** `grounding-format.md` §8 now splits the outcome by origin: an own-run finding, which nothing cites yet, is still rewritten under the same id, while an on-file finding is superseded — its on-file verdict kept as `prior_verdict` — and a successor carrying the re-derived verdict is appended with the next id. The in-place rewrite left a decision citing the id standing on a verdict it was never taken on, with nothing reading `SUPERSEDED` for `product-workflows`' `/brd-interview` to see, and a later supersession then recorded the rewritten verdict as the one the decision was taken on. §2.1 and §6.3 name both routes. On a `[DG#n]`, either write keeps the frame citations of the evidence it replaces, so a design finding can always be placed in its frame set. §5 now applies the same rule to a horizon: a grounding run never moves a written finding's `horizon` or `prerequisite` in place, and supersedes it with a successor carrying the new one. Where the run cannot give a successor what its verifier needs, it supersedes the finding with none and says in the note which horizon it would have written, so no stale horizon is left standing. The successor field lists in §5 and §8 name `class` and any `cites` on a `[DG#n]`, `consumed_by: none`, and, in §8, a `control` where the successor owes one. **A `contradict` whose return is incomplete — the finding owes a control and no `own_control` came back — writes no `outcome` at all** (§2.1, §8): an own-run finding stops the verifying run before it writes any finding, and an on-file block keeps every field it holds, the run reporting it as not verified. §2's `prior_verdict` row now says a finding already reading `SUPERSEDED` is never marked again by any rule of any run, so the field can never record `SUPERSEDED`. §6.3's class-4 cascade names every route that replaces a cited `[CG#n]`'s verdict: the in-place rewrite of an own-run finding, and a supersession, whether written by a re-grounding run, by §8's handling of an on-file finding or by §5's horizon rule.
- **`grounding-format` §4 records a pin with the findings pinned to it** (`product-workflows` 3.8.3, R74). Step 1 appended `baselines.md` as the pin was taken, so a run that stopped before writing its findings left a new pin standing over the old findings. It now says the pin is recorded in the same write as the findings pinned to it, never before them, so a stopped run leaves the previously recorded pin in place.
- **`phase-handoff` §3.4's `/prd-ground` ledger row split row F further** (`product-workflows` 3.8.3, xi, N7). A slice's `coverage-ledger.md` in the folder and on no ref was always `PRD_GROUND_NOT_HANDED_OFF`, a declined handoff, although an interrupted `/brd-split` carve leaves the same state. The row now reads the parent's ledger first. It stops with `PRD_GROUND_CARVE_UNFINISHED` where that ledger still holds an `unallocated` row, and with `PRD_GROUND_SLICE_UNRECONCILED` where the slice's `brd-link.md` names no `parent:`, where that ledger cannot be read, or where the slice does not agree with it. Only otherwise does it stop with `PRD_GROUND_NOT_HANDED_OFF`. Where the slice's inventory is also missing from the folder, the inventory's own `PRD_GROUND_NO_INVENTORY` prints instead. The inventory row now says a parent re-run never writes into a slice with a file missing and never re-creates one.
- **`phase-handoff` gates the two files `/brd-package` ships beside the register** (`product-workflows` 3.8.3, N16). §4.0 classed `interview/customer-questions.md` and `code-defect-log.md` advisory, so `/brd-package` took the register's merge as evidence that both had merged too, the sibling inference §4.0 forbids. A handoff declined and landed in part, or a later `/brd-interview` run that stages the register and not a file an earlier run left, lands `decisions.md` alone. A new §3.4 row gates each file where it is in the folder, stopping with `BRD_PACKAGE_SHIPPED_NOT_HANDED_OFF`. §4.0 now classes both files gated. `/brd-package`'s round-record row adds `BRD_PACKAGE_ROUND_NOT_RECORDED`, for a round whose record is not even in the folder, and says the round set is never derived from a torn write, which `/brd-package` refuses first with `BRD_PACKAGE_TORN_WRITES`.
- **`addressing`'s `revisions/` register and `phase-handoff`'s unread class now name `/create-prd`'s archive** (`product-workflows` 3.8.3, N22). `/create-prd` now archives an existing `prd.md` into `revisions/` before it overwrites the file. §2's register of reserved subfolders names it as a second writer beside `/update-prd`, and §4.0 and §4.3 class the archived PRD revision of either command unread. §4.0's proposal row names an archived proposal or brief at the first free `-2`, `-3` name `product-workflows:proposal-format` §2 falls to, as well as at the dated one.
- **`escalation-rules`' `key dir not found` rule is scoped to an address that resolved to no folder** (vi, N25). It was also used where the resolved folder "holds no artifact the run needs" and where a command rejected a key failing `addressing` §1's grammar. In both cases the key had resolved, or had never been a key, so re-entering it could not help. The rule now covers `status: absent`, and a folder that resolved earlier in the run but is gone when a later phase reads it. It says a folder that is there is never this case, and names `/document`'s and `/release-notes`' `*_BRD_NOT_SLICED`, `*_FOLDER_NOT_PLACED`, `*_PRD_NO_KEY` and `*_NO_PRD` stops (`docs-workflows` 1.3.4) as the cases it does not cover. The grammar-failure clause is deleted: `dev-workflows` 4.2.4's `/ready` and `docs-workflows` 1.3.4's `/release-notes` now stop on `status: invalid` with their own `*_NEEDS_KEY`, and no caller is left for it.
- **`specs-repo-git` placed the preflight "as early as `$SPECS_PATH` is known", which the commands that resolve an address first no longer do** (R69, R79). A command that takes an address and reads its key set from resolution resolves it first, since the run key set §3.2 hands the preflight is what resolution returns, and runs the preflight before any placement or refusal reads the resolved folder; where resolution comes first, a run that stops on its address runs none. A command whose key set is its argument keys as typed — `dev-workflows` 4.2.4's `/vuln`, one per token — runs it before resolving. §3 and §7's caller contract now say so, §3.2 no longer says no command defers its preflight to obtain a key, and the repo-root `CLAUDE.md` states the same placement. **§3.5 gains a same-run re-run rule**: where a command runs the preflight again with a changed key set — `docs-workflows` 1.3.4's `/document` and `/release-notes`, on a slice key entered at their BRD-container stop — and the earlier run's B4 switched away from a plugin branch `branch-key` now resolves to the new set, the preflight switches back to it first, then runs stages 1–3, whose B3 stays on it; a failed switch is reported and the run continues. Both commands cite the rule rather than carrying a switch of their own.
- **`grounding-format` §6.3 fixes a class-1 finding's claim form** (`product-workflows` 3.8.3, R80). A frame showing a field no requirement asks for has no requirement id to open its `claim` with, so a later finding could not be matched to it. Class 1 now writes `claim: none — frame-only: <field>`, `<field>` being that one field's label exactly as the frame displays it — one finding per field, told apart by the token, which `design-grounder` writes and `/brd-interview`'s successor test matches on.
- **`/frames` read the resolved folder before its specs-repo preflight** (R81). Its carrier test reads an `@<path>` folder for carrier files, and the preflight came after model routing. The preflight now runs at the end of step 1's resolution, on `status: found`, with the resolved key and, on an Epic folder, its parent carrier's key as the run key set, before the carrier and kind tests; a run that stops on its address runs none. The docs page says so.
- **`next-phase-offer` offered another `/brd-interview` round wherever `/brd-reconcile` reopened a decision** (`product-workflows` 3.8.3, R32). While a round stays open, that command resumes it, and it puts a reopened decision's question only in a round it opens. `/brd-reconcile`'s re-entry list now makes the offer where the run reopened a decision, unless a round stays open holding only questions held for the customer; a round open on a *deferred*, *untagged* or *needs grounding* question is one that run resumes.

## [1.7.5] — 2026-09-23

**Update `dev-workflows`, `product-workflows` and `docs-workflows` with this release**: their model-routing pages and command placeholders name the chain's new head.

### Added
- **Claude Opus 5.5 heads the powerful-model chain.** `model-routing/classification.md` §2 now resolves `claude-opus-5-5` first, ahead of `claude-opus-5`, and its handoff-block examples name it.
- **`cost-prices.yaml` prices Opus 5.5 at its standard rates**, from Anthropic's pricing page: $4 input, $20 output, $0.20 cache read, $5 5-minute and $8 1-hour cache writes per million tokens. Its cache reads bill at 0.05x input, not the usual 0.1x, which the header and `cost-emission.md` now say.

### Fixed — behaviour
- **Every Opus 5.5 turn was priced at Opus 5's rates.** The price engine falls back to the longest table key that prefixes a model id, and `claude-opus-5` prefixes `claude-opus-5-5`, so with no key of its own Opus 5.5 was costed about 25% high, with no `unpriced-model` note to show it. The new key fixes it; `session-cost.py --selftest` now pins exact-key pricing for an id that extends another key, and `cost-emission.md`'s add-a-model steps say a price entry is not optional for such an id.

## [1.7.4] — 2026-09-23

### Changed
- The design-spec citations in the `addressing`, `grounding-format` and `implementation-format` references, and the one in `scripts/session-cost.py`'s docstring, now say the spec was removed from the tree on 2026-09-23 and name the `git show 62e791e8:<path>` that retrieves it. `read-only-repos` no longer names an internal repository in its read-only-mount measurement. Wording only, no behaviour.

### Fixed — behaviour

- **`grounding-format` §2 gains `prior_verdict`, the verdict a finding carried when it was superseded.** §3's `SUPERSEDED` overwrote `verdict` and kept nothing of it, and `product-workflows` 3.8.1's `/brd-interview` now reopens a `decided` record a `--rebaseline` pass moved the ground under unless the successor findings confirm its premise — a test that needs the verdict each superseded finding carried, to tell a re-grounding that came back as it stood from one that moved the ground. The field is required beside `verdict: SUPERSEDED` and omitted otherwise (§2.1); it is history, not a second verdict, so §2.1's closed field set admits it as one of §2's fields without either route gate changing. A finding superseded before the field existed carries none, and a reader treats its prior verdict as unknown, never as matching. §3's `SUPERSEDED` row names the field, and `docs/reference/references.md`'s entry for this file mentions it.
- **`phase-handoff` §3.4 gains a row for the BRD route's decision register.** `/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` read a slice's `decisions.md` ungated from the working tree, so a declined or unmerged `/brd-reconcile` or `/brd-interview` handoff let a PRD, ARD or specification be authored against `[VD#n]`/`[CD#n]` records nobody had merged. The three now run `require-on-main` on it (`product-workflows` 3.8.1), and the new row states what each does: a register absent from the folder falls back exactly as before — reported, never a stop — while one in the folder and on no ref stops with the command's own `*_REGISTER_NOT_HANDED_OFF` id, beside rows D/E and the C rows. §3.4's build-ladder paragraph says the row is BRD-route only, its behaviour-change paragraph names it, and the list of rows that stop on `absent` outright now says this one does not. **No consent array moves**: `decisions.md` was already gated, and already *stopping*, through `/brd-package`'s row, so `/brd-interview`, `/brd-reconcile`'s second handoff, `/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` keep §4.3's gated-stopping array. The consumer set is unchanged too — all three already ran `require-on-main` on the PRD or `idea.md`.
- **`escalation-rules`' closed-vocabulary pickers gain `/brd-reconcile`'s conflicting-answer picker**, new in `product-workflows` 3.8.1 — freeze this candidate, keep the earlier one, or ask the customer, the one not frozen set aside as a conflicting answer and never frozen — so the pickers whose free-text answer is normalised or re-asked number nine, and the ninth joins the first three as a picker through which a customer's authority enters `decisions.md`. The propagation-sweep row, which named `reopened` withheld only on an `[AS#n]` item and `reverted` only where the prior position is not preserved, now also names `reopened` withheld on any decision not `decided`, `reverted` withheld where the restored evidence fires the will-change rule unresolved, and an item whose record is `superseded` or `withdrawn` not presented at all, as that release's sweep does.
- **`next-phase-offer` said none of `/brd-reconcile`'s three advance offers carries `<merge-clause>`**, because none gated anything that command writes. With the register gated, all three do, and the clause resolves from the reconciliation record's handoff line — the multi-handoff rule now names them beside `/brd-package` and `/prd-ground`.
- **`escalation-rules`' *Required path environment variable unset* citers and `specs-repo-git` §3.1's unset-`$SPECS_PATH` classes now include `/release-notes`**, which stops on an unset variable for a `<KEY>` address (Phase 0 step 1, `docs-workflows` 1.3.2) where it used to fall through to a not-found stop offering a re-enter that could not succeed. §3.1 keeps it in the not-found class only for an `@<path>` address; `/epics` stays there on both forms.
- **`phase-handoff` §3.4's route-command paragraph now names the Epic-folder refusals.** It listed which folders `/brd-interview`, `/brd-package`, `/brd-reconcile` and `/brd-split` refuse before their gates; an `EPIC-` folder, which `resolve-address` also finds, is now refused by the five route commands §3.4 gates (`*_EPIC_LEVEL` — four of them new in `product-workflows` 3.8.1, `/prd-ground`'s older), and the paragraph says so. `/brd-intake`, which §3.4 does not gate, refuses one too. No row's disposition changes.
- **`/frames` refused a spec folder with no carrier as "not a BRD, PRD or Epic folder".** A legacy folder whose artifacts assert no `kind:` or `key:` resolves with an empty `kind` — by key through §5's fallback, which now keeps such a folder, and by `@<path>` — and the kind test sent the operator to the folder above it, while that test and Phase 4's cost attribution need a kind and, on an `@<path>`, the index, the `frames/` branch and the commit subject need a key. Phase 0 now tests for such a folder on positive evidence — a `<KEY>` run's record with `kind` empty, or an `@<path>` run's with `kind` empty on a folder holding one of the files §5 names as a carrier — and stops with `FRAMES_NO_CARRIER`, naming the carrier files it holds and how to give one of them `key:` and its folder kind — or, on a `<KEY>` run's folder holding none of them, §5's advice to rename a pre-rename `<KEY>_<slug>.md` to `prd.md` with `kind: prd` and `key:`, or otherwise to add the carrier file its level takes — and `/product-workflows:create-prd <KEY>` where the folder holds an `idea.md` and no `prd.md`, on an `@<path>` run only where the folder's name carries no kind prefix, since `/create-prd <KEY>` cannot find a prefixed folder asserting no key and would create a second. An `@<path>` to a directory asserting nothing and holding none of those files — a frame set not yet indexed — still reaches the kind test. Phase 4's list of Phase 0 stops also said they all refuse "before a folder is resolved"; several resolve one first, and it now says they stop before the run has a spec folder whose kind and key it can index. `docs/commands/frames.md` names the stop.

### Fixed — claims the wider exclusivity probe found false

- **`next-phase-offer` said "only two" of the `<merge-clause>` table's rows name a branch**, a paragraph after saying three do. It now says three.
- **`next-phase-offer`'s scope paragraph said the offers it binds span `product-workflows`, `dev-workflows` and `workflows-core`.** `workflows-core` prints none, and `docs-workflows` prints several. It now names the three plugins that print offers and says `workflows-core` prints none; the check-11 measurement paragraph, which called `product-workflows` "the plugin the scope paragraph above now qualifies", now calls it the plugin whose `/brd-*` and `/prd-*` families are the gate's scope.
- **`next-phase-offer` said "two further commands" match the family globs** where one of the two, `/brd-proposal`, is named earlier in the same paragraph. It now says both proposal commands match them.
- **`phase-handoff` §3.7 carried a stray clause** — "; are exhaustive for the non-stopping rows only" — left from an earlier rewrite. It is deleted.
- **`specs-repo-git` said every Phase 0 stop in the family comes before a branch.** `/document` in keyed mode can cut a docs-repository branch and commit the profile on it in Phase 0, before its later Phase 0 stops. The paragraph now says most do, names that one, and leaves such a run's tail to its command, as it already did for a later stop.
- **`/statusline`, `cost-emission`, `feedback-emission`, `cost-prices.yaml`, the status-line script's comments, the `model-routing` skill's description and the three `/prompt*` commands called themselves `dev-workflows`'** status line, cost subsystem, feedback emitter or commands. All ship from `workflows-core` and serve the whole family; each now says the plugin family's. Paths and commit-subject strings keep the old name.
- **`source-truth` called its rule one of the most important in "the `dev-workflows` plugin".** It ships from `workflows-core` and its consumers include `docs-workflows`' agents and `/release-notes`; it now says the plugin family.
