- **`grounding-format` said neither index writer "consults an index".** `/frames` reads an existing index to preserve its descriptions; what neither does is consult one as evidence, which is what the sentence now says.
- **`implementation-format` said a `release-notes.md` written before `docs-workflows` 1.2.2 is "every one that exists today".** The clause is deleted.
- **`source-truth` §1, §2 and §3.0 named a spec layout nothing writes** — the PRD spec markdown, child Epic specs, `requirements.md` and `tasks.md`. They now name the layout the family writes: `specification.md` and `design.md` at the PRD folder and each `EPIC-*/` subfolder, and each Epic's `epic.md`, with `prd.md` as the corroborating PRD; §3.0's `grep` keeps those three names rather than excluding two others.
- **`prd-format` said `/update-prd` "reads no BRD tree".** Its Phase 0 opens a slice's `brd-link.md` and ledger to word a stop's remedy; the sentence now says it reads nothing of a BRD tree as a source for `brd_key`, `brd_parent` or `depends_on`. It also said `workitem_key` is "never written by the plugin", while the three code-changing commands write it into a `Work-Item:` commit trailer; it now says never written into frontmatter, and names the trailer.
- **`docs/reference/environment.md` said `dev-workflows` reads all four of the path and identity variables.** It never reads `$DOCS_PATH`; the page now says it reads three.
- **`docs/reference/references.md` said it enumerates "every file a command or agent cites by name"**, and counted 27. The two files under `handoff/` are cited by name as well; the page now says it enumerates every file except those two.
- **Pre-feature "as today" wording** in `docs-grounder`, `docs-grounding`, `feedback-emission` and `phase-handoff` now reads "as it does without" the feature in question, since "today" had come to read as the present.
- **`addressing.md`'s reserved-subdirectory register** called itself "the register of names resolution must pass over" and omitted four: `revisions/`, `bundle-<YYYYMMDD>/`, `customer-sent-<YYYYMMDD>/` and `/document`'s `Doc screenshots/`. None broke resolution, since none matches the `*-<KEY>-*` glob, but the register claim was false; each is now listed with its owner cited. The register's lead, *each reserved name is defined once, elsewhere, and cited here*, was also false for `brd/`, `grounding/`, `interview/` and `dev-workflows/`, which were listed with no owner; each now cites one — `product-workflows:brd-format`, `grounding-format` §2, `/product-workflows:brd-interview` Phase 9 and `specs-repo-git` §2.1.
- `specs-repo-git`'s reason no directory shape stages a `/document` screenshot copy was that the run "may stage into an existing `Attachments/` subfolder holding the operator's own files". `docs-workflows` 1.3.2 stops staging there — the default is always `Doc screenshots/` — so the reason now names the case that remains: a staging directory the operator names at `/document`'s Phase 1, which may sit under `$SPECS_PATH` beside their own files.
- **`phase-handoff` §3.4's `/brd-split` and `/brd-interview` rows named only `*_NEEDS_GROUNDING` for row F**, where each command splits it on the inventory into a grounding stop, a missing-inventory stop and an empty-inventory stop. Both rows now name the split — `/brd-interview`'s also its no-parent form — and say that no stop after the grounding one names `/prd-ground` as the fix. The route-commands paragraph now says the `(a slice)` qualification names the level a row serves — a slice a BRD carved — that `/prd-ground`'s two BRD-route rows and `/brd-interview`'s row also dispose of a folder that is neither a slice nor a container, which their earlier tests let through, and names the new idea-route refusals.
- **`phase-handoff` §3.4's `/prd-ground` row called `PRD_GROUND_NEEDS_INTAKE`'s folder "the legacy unprefixed folder that reads as an interrupted intake".** The stop now describes a folder that is neither a slice nor a BRD container and names a fresh `/brd-intake` under a new key; the row says so, and the `/brd-interview` row now says its no-parent stop names a fresh intake rather than a re-run over the same folder. The `/prd-ground` inventory row said `PRD_GROUND_NO_INVENTORY` names "the producer by level"; its remedy turns on the slice's `claims:` and the parent's ledger — the empty-inventory remedy for a slice claiming nothing; for one claiming rows, where the inventory was never written — no ledger in its folder, and none of its claimed rows `covered-by` it on the parent's ledger — removing that slice folder — which no command has committed; where it was committed by hand, from every branch carrying it, the default branch included — and re-running `/brd-split` on the parent with an instruction while a claimed row is still `unallocated`, or emptying the left-over `claims:` by hand and re-running the parent in the form that ledger names where none is, and where it was lost after it was written a restore from the ref that carried it; no `/brd-split` form where that ledger cannot be read — and on a folder naming no `parent:` a fresh intake under a new key, and the row now says so.
- **`addressing` §5 said a pre-prefix tree held only a BRD slice one level inside its parent.** A per-Epic subfolder sat there too, and the two-level fallback resolves both; the sentence now names it.
- **`addressing` §5 did not say what its legacy fallback matches on.** §3 step 2 keeps only a prefixed candidate asserting `key: <KEY>`, and a pre-prefix folder usually asserts nothing — `idea.md` gained `kind:`/`key:` on 2026-09-01, a day after the prefixes — so applied to the fallback that filter would have left every such folder unaddressable. §5 now says it matches the folder name, as the pre-prefix resolver did (`git show 8e2ec7b9^:plugins/dev-workflows/references/brd-addressing.md` §2), top level first and one level deeper only where the top level kept nothing, and drops only a candidate whose carrier asserts another key. On a folder with no carrier reached by key, the resolution record's `key` is the key searched for and its `kind` is empty, and §4's key-versus-name stop does not fire; the record's comments and §4's *never keyless* rule say so. §4.1 now says its stop on a folder none of its three rules places binds a caller that needs the folder placed: one that refuses only the levels it does not work at proceeds on the rest, and where the file it writes asserts `kind: prd` beside the key, that file places the folder from then on — `/product-workflows:create-prd` and `/product-workflows:idea` are two such callers, each stating its own test.
- **`addressing` said nothing of an `@<path>` to a folder with no carrier, and its migration advice made such folders.** §3 step 1 read the folder's `kind:` and `key:` with no searched key to fall back on; it now says both come back empty, and that a caller needing the key stops, naming the folder and the two ways on — give it a carrier, or address it by its `<KEY>` where its name is still unprefixed. §5 told a user renaming a legacy folder to its prefixed name that one `git mv` did it: on a folder with no carrier that rename made it unaddressable by key, because §3 step 2 drops a prefixed candidate asserting nothing and the fallback no longer matches the name. §5 now says to add `key:` and the folder kind to the artifact that becomes the carrier in the same commit, and that a `<KEY>_<slug>.md` renamed to `prd.md` also takes `kind: prd` and `key:` — the fields arrived in the same commit as the keyless filenames — since a `prd.md` asserting no `kind:` places nothing and reads as no PRD to `/epics`, `/prd-ground` and `/brd-split`. §4.1's stop on a folder none of its rules places now also names `/product-workflows:create-prd <KEY>` where the folder holds an `idea.md` and no `prd.md` — from a caller for which it is a way on: one working at the PRD level, which the placing lets re-run, or a BRD-route command refusing the folder, which names it as the idea route's next step; `/product-workflows:brd-intake` names no command there. `dev-workflows` 4.2.2's `/design`, `/implement` and `/ready`, `docs-workflows` 1.3.2's `/document` and `/release-notes`, and `product-workflows` 3.8.1's `/create-ard` and `/specify` and the no-parent stops of its `/brd-interview` and `/brd-split` and the two no-link stops of its `/prd-ground` (`PRD_GROUND_NO_INVENTORY` on a folder naming no `parent:`, and `PRD_GROUND_NEEDS_INTAKE`) name it; §4.1 names the BRD-route ones as examples, "among them".
- **`phase-handoff` §3.4's `/prd-ground` rows** now head a missing inventory or ledger *never produced, or lost after it was written*, and narrow `PRD_GROUND_INVENTORY_NOT_HANDED_OFF` to a folder holding a `coverage-ledger.md` too, as `product-workflows` 3.8.1's `/prd-ground` does.

## [1.7.3] — 2026-09-22

### Changed — `phase-handoff`'s unread register no longer lists `/specify`'s rendered `.html`

`product-workflows` 3.8.0 stops `/specify` writing it, so §4.0's register row and §4.3's list of the `unread` class's members both drop it. No array a producer selects changes: `/specify`'s deliverable set still holds `specification.md`, a gated path, and §4.0's strongest-class rule selects the gated array.

### Fixed — claims the wider exclusivity probe found false

- **`phase-handoff` §3.7 said `/ready` is the sole caller that reads a stopping row without stopping.** `/brd-reconcile` does too, at two sites: its cross-BRD write guard runs `require-on-main` against another BRD's artifact and, on a stopping row or row F, records the write and continues; and on a `--sent` run its Phase 0 step 6 runs the gate only as a redundancy check, where a stopping row proceeds and a pass stops (`BRD_RECONCILE_SENT_REDUNDANT`). §3.7 now names both callers and both sites, and §3.4's `/brd-reconcile` row, which said `--sent` *replaces* the gate, now says what the command executes: the gate still runs where the folder holds a prompt, with its reading inverted.
- **`phase-handoff` §3.4 said "all six `/brd-*` consumers" sit outside rule 3's qualification**, listing `/prd-ground` (not a `/brd-*` command) and omitting `/prd-proposal`, whose `prd.md` row stops on `absent` because its input shipped with it. It now names the seven by that property — every stopping row but `/design`'s, whose stop predates the gate.
- **`implementation-format` said the only runs that end uncommitted are those that typed `--no-commit`.** A failed `code-handoff` gate or a hook rejection also ends uncommitted, each on a `NOT committed` line, as does a repository a multi-source `/implement` run edited and never branched. It now names all three.
- **`followup-emission` listed five callers.** Ten commands load it — `/docs-init`, `/docs-brand`, `/docs-audit`, `/prd-proposal` and `/brd-proposal` as well. It now gives the `grep` that returns them, and the ten it returns today.
- **`specs-repo-git` §2.1 named `/document`, `/release-notes` and the ● marker as `implementation.md`'s readers.** `/ready`, `/epics` and `/specify` read it too. It now names all of them, with the `grep` that finds them.
- **`branch-naming` claimed every command that creates a git branch.** A branch in `$SPECS_PATH` is named by `phase-handoff` §2.2; the file now scopes itself to code and docs repositories and says so.
- **`grounding-format` §6.1 said a missing frame-set index is recoverable "in exactly one way", and two paragraphs later "unrecoverable".** `/frames` writes one and a hand-written index serves as well; both sentences now say a missing index is a refusal until one is written. `/frames`' command body and its docs page carried the "unrecoverable" wording and now match.
- **`grilling-technique` counted eight callers at three depths**, and called `/brd-split` "the one caller" that is both grilling and never-batch, though every caller is never-batch. Ten commands grill — `/prd-proposal` and `/brd-proposal` for their proposal profile as well — at two depths; `/brd-split` is now described as a caller whose *own* rule forbids batching. Its **Relentless** list, which still named five, now names the proposal pair as well, making seven relentless callers beside the three bounded ones. `dependencies.md`'s summary of the same count now matches.
- **`specs-repo-git` said "nothing mints anything now"** where `/brd-split` mints slice keys and `/epics` mints Epic keys. The claim it needed is narrower and now reads that no handoff mints a key; a neighbouring "Nothing mints it", about the PRD's `key`, now says `/create-prd` copies it and mints nothing.
- **`cost-emission` §11 counted five `inferred` commands and named five.** §7 has six — `/frames` resolves from the folder's own `kind` — and both of §11's enumerations now include it.
- **`dependencies.md` called `prose-style` "the fallback below the repo's own linter" in `docs-workflows`.** `docs-style-checker` dispatches it on every run — complementary, fallback or sole — and `/release-notes` runs it directly. The row now says so.
- **`prd-format` said "no command consumes" `brd_key`, `brd_parent` and `depends_on`.** `/brd-proposal` takes slice order from `depends_on`. The claim is now scoped to the build ladder, and the `/brd-proposal` read is named beside `prd-reviewer`'s integrity check on the pair.
- **`docs/reference/references.md` described its addressing section as holding "the one artifact format" that is shared.** It holds two — `prd-format.md` and `grounding-format.md` — and now says "formats".
- **The `model-routing` skill's description counted 25 pipeline commands.** 26 invoke it; `/docs-audit` was missing, and is now listed.
- **`grounding-format` §6.2's reconciliation contract called a frame no index identifies "§6.1's own unrecoverable failure".** §6.1 now makes a missing index a refusal, recovered by writing one. The contract now calls it the state §6.1 makes a refusal.
- **`phase-handoff` §4.0 defined the `unread` class as "no reader at all"**, while its own register row admits `proposal-reviewer`'s same-run read of `proposal-brief.md`. The class is now "no reader after the run that wrote it — none downstream, and no later run of the producing command", which is what §4.3 already asks a producer to look for; §4.3's own "nothing reads it" carries the same qualifier.
- **`next-phase-offer` said `/create-ard` and `/specify` on a slice "neither reads the ledger".** Both open it for their next-step offers and Final reports. It now says neither reads it as an authoring input, which is what the offer condition turns on.
- **`addressing`'s opening named the commands that reach the tree through it**, and missed `/vuln`, `/implement` and `/brd-proposal`, all of which call `resolve-address`. It now gives `grep -l resolve-address plugins/*/commands/*.md` in place of a list or a count.
- **`addressing` §7 said "Adopted in twelve files. Eleven commands cite this section"** and kept a *Not adopters* list. No command cites §7 to reach the fallback — each calls `resolve-address` (§3), which carries §5's fallback — and `/implement`, `/vuln` and `/brd-proposal` resolve that way without a row in the table. §7 now says every command that addresses a folder reaches the tree through §3, gives the same `grep` for the set, keeps no total, calls its eleven-row table a finding aid for where eleven of those commands resolve rather than the set, folds *Not adopters* away, and names `/implement` among `ard-resolution.md`'s six delegators. Its *Adoption is additive* paragraph, which `/brd-split` cites by name, is kept, and the section's account of the retired count now says no command cites §7 *to reach the fallback*, `/brd-split` citing only that paragraph. The colon that introduced the table as though it listed the commands absent from it is now a full stop.
- **`ard-resolution` said `/implement` resolves no PRD folder at all.** On a keyed run it resolves its own positional address with `resolve-address`; what holds is that it reaches an ARD only through this file. The sentence now says that.
- **`branch-naming` said `/vuln`'s branch name is applied by `vuln-fixer`.** `/vuln`'s orchestrator resolves it once per CVE in its Step 1 and hands it to the agent, which never derives one. The consumer entry now says so.
- **`dependencies.md` said `docs-workflows`' "six commands and eight agents" read the shared corpus.** It has seven commands, all of which do, and eight of its eleven agents do. It now says that, with the `grep` that re-derives it. Its "nothing at run time reads a sibling" now reads "probes for": runs do dispatch `prose-style-checker`, a sibling's agent, but as a declared dependency the host installs, never one a run looks for.
- **`escalation-rules` named six citers for which `SPECS_PATH` is the only path variable.** `/brd-split` and `/brd-intake` qualify too. The clause now states the property — every citer but `/prd-ground` — instead of a subset.
- **`grounding-format`'s re-derivation recipe searched only commands and agents, and said a §6 change would land under "three files that were never asked".** Shared references cite it as well, and the recipe returns far more than three. The recipe now includes `references/`, and the count is gone.
- **`phase-handoff` §3.4 listed five build-ladder commands that stop with a `*_BRD_NOT_SLICED` id.** `/design`, `/implement` and `/ready` stop with one too, and each takes that refusal before its gate. The paragraph now gives the `grep` for the set, lists all eight, and says every one of them but `/create-prd` refuses before the gate.
- **`phase-handoff` §4.1 said "the three producers above may reach no offer".** A producer can reach none in more ways than three — `/brd-proposal` writes nothing where its readiness walk ends on *Price the slice first* or *Re-price it first*, and any run that stops before its handoff phase reaches none. That case is added to the list, and the sentence now calls the list examples.
- **`phase-handoff` §4.0 named five readers of `brd-link.md`.** Most of the family's folder-level tests read it. The row now calls those five examples and gives the `grep` for the set.
- **`phase-handoff` §4.0's `code-defect-log.md` row named two readers.** `/brd-reconcile`, `/prd-proposal`'s defect sweep and `proposal-reviewer` read it too. The row now names them and gives the recipe, and its "every reader above says so" is scoped to the readers that do.
- **`prd-format` called `/document`'s and `/release-notes`' commit grep `workitem_key`'s "one consumer".** `/implement`, `/vuln` and `/upgrade` write it into their commits as a `Work-Item:` trailer (`code-handoff` §2.3). The paragraph now names both consumers.
- **`specs-repo-git` §3.2 and §3.6 listed five Epic-scoped commands.** Every command that takes an Epic address runs Epic-scoped — `/implement`, `/document`, `/release-notes` and `/epics` among them. Both lists are gone, and "four of the five read the PRD above" now says only that not every command does, `/frames` being one that does not.
- **`classification.md` §8 said it is "consulted by `/implement`".** Seven commands run it, three of which also adopt §8.5. The opening now points at §8.5's *Opt-in* paragraph, which already names them.
- **`next-phase-offer` said `workflows-core` "prints one offer of its own, `/frames`".** The same file says `/frames` makes no next-phase offer, only its §4.3 handoff offer. The sentence now says that.
- **The README's list of what a sibling plugin reads left out the `reference` loader skill**, which is how that plugin reads every one of the 29 reference files. It is now in the list.
- **`docs/roles-and-phases.md` counted "thirteen phases — twelve lifecycle"**, and said the other ten are each documented by the plugin that emits them. §7 has thirteen lifecycle phases, `docs-audit` among them, and `docs-workflows` has no roles page. The page now points at §7 and keeps no count, and names `documenting`, `docs-scaffold` and `docs-audit` as phases §7 alone defines.
- **`docs/getting-started.md` said `$DOCS_PATH` is "never written to".** `docs-workflows` writes into it where it resolves `$DOCS_PATH` as the docs repository it edits. The sentence is now scoped to its use as a grounding root.
- **`docs/reference/session-feedback.md` spoke of "the four commands in this plugin"** as its feedback emitters, but `/frames` emits end-of-run feedback too. The page now says "the four feedback commands" and names `/frames` as a fifth emitter.
- **`docs/workflow.md` said each of the six commands is charged to a phase.** `/statusline` emits no cost entry. The sentence now says five, and names the exception.
- **`specs-repo-git` §3.1 said a failed gate is "not one disposition but two"** — a read-only `.git`, and a set path that is no repository — and gave the third state, `$SPECS_PATH` **unset**, no disposition at all. It now names it: a silent no-op at this step, because whether an unset path is supported is the caller's to say before it gets here — a command that resolves or creates its deliverable's folder by key stops in Phase 0 (the `grep` for them is given), `/epics` and `/release-notes` stop not-found on a key and run on with an `@<path>` into the folder given, and one for which the specs repository holds only bookkeeping runs on (`/implement` on a direct prompt and `/docs-init`, `/docs-brand` and `/docs-audit` now named among them).
- **`escalation-rules` *Required path environment variable unset*** now lists `/idea`, which `product-workflows` 3.8.0 gives that stop: `/idea` wrote its deliverable under `$SPECS_PATH/specifications/` with no unset check, so an unset variable aimed the write at `/specifications/` under the filesystem root.
- **`next-phase-offer` said `/create-prd` "refuses three shapes and not one"**; `product-workflows` 3.8.0 adds a fourth, an `EPIC-` folder on either route, so the sentence now says so. Its fallback offer named `/create-prd <ADDRESS>` where the caller's resolved folder holds no PRD; on an Epic-level run that is the Epic's key, which `/create-prd` refuses. It now tests the PRD folder — the parent on an Epic-level run — and names that folder's own key, `<PRD>`.
- **`escalation-rules` said none of the other citers of *Required path environment variable unset* "opens a repository".** `/idea --ground-code` reads `${REPOS_PATH:-/workspace}`. It now says none of the others requires a repository root, and says `/idea` reads the default.
- **`docs/roles-and-phases.md` said `docs-workflows`' phases are defined by §7's rows "alone".** That plugin's session-cost reference page says which of its commands emits each; the page now points there.
- **`specs-repo-git` §3.1's unset-`$SPECS_PATH` disposition left two callers in no class.** Keyed `/implement` and keyed `/document` resolve a folder by address and write into it (`implementation.md`; `pr-draft.md` and the implementation-gaps draft) and carry no unset stop, while the bookkeeping-only class named only their direct-mode runs. On a `<KEY>` address they now sit with the commands that stop on the unset variable — `dev-workflows` 4.2.1 and `docs-workflows` 1.3.1 give both that stop, checked before the key is resolved — and on an `@<path>` address with `/epics` and `/release-notes`, running on and writing into the folder given. The bullet also says its three classes are every caller of the entry point.
- **`escalation-rules` *Required path environment variable unset*** now lists `/implement` (Phase 0 *Address resolution*) and `/document` (*Mode detection*, keyed mode), each on a `<KEY>` address only — the stop `dev-workflows` 4.2.1 and `docs-workflows` 1.3.1 give them. Its note on why `SPECS_PATH` is the only variable its citers require now also names `/document`'s defaulted `${REPOS_PATH:-/workspace}` and the `$DOCS_PATH` rung of its docs-repository ladder, neither of which stops a run.
- **`specs-repo-git`'s *Run key set* said §3.5 must recognise a branch named for either key twice in one sentence.** Once now.

## [1.7.2] — 2026-09-22

### Changed — the shared authorities admit `/docs-audit`, which `docs-workflows` 1.3.0 ships

Nine files here name the family's commands, and a new one in `docs-workflows` had to reach each of them; none of this changes behaviour for any existing caller. `scripts/command-namespaces.json` gains the command, so it resolves under its plugin namespace. `references/cost-emission.md` gains its fixed `docs-audit`/`dev` §7 row and names it in §8's rung 2, the documentation branch that files a run with no PRD folder under `$SPECS_PATH/documentation/<docs-repo-slug>/` (D19) — the rung's own count moves with it. `references/feedback-emission.md` does the same for its tier-2 branch and its counts. `references/finding-triage.md` records `docs-audit-reviewer` → the orchestrator in its attachment table and in the no-fixer paragraph beside `docs-scaffold-reviewer` and `proposal-reviewer`: this command has no fixer agent either, so its orchestrator applies survivors itself (D25). `references/model-routing/classification.md` adds the command to §8's fan-out adopters and to §8.5's opt-in seeded second round, and says where its unresolved themes land — `docs-auditor`'s `unresolved[]`, never a gap. `references/next-phase-offer.md` adds it to *Not pipeline nodes*, a run that prints the universal-minimum prose `### Next step` and carries no `<merge-clause>`, because the backlog it leaves is in a docs repository's working tree and no `$SPECS_PATH` gate reads it. `references/specs-repo-git.md` names it among the runs whose write target is not a git work tree, where the docs-repo slug falls back to a basename. `agents/code-scanner.md` and `docs/reference/agents.md` add it to that agent's caller list: `/docs-audit` dispatches one scanner per repository the docs profile records.

**Update this plugin with `docs-workflows` 1.3.0.** Nothing here is required by an older `docs-workflows`, but that release's `/docs-audit` reads all nine.

## [1.7.1] — 2026-09-22

### Fixed — the `<merge-clause>` table resolved six of `phase-handoff` §4.1's eight outcome rows, and the two it dropped both lost what the run was holding

`next-phase-offer.md`'s resolution table named six outcomes and sent everything else to `(once this phase's artifacts are on the default branch)`. Two of §4.1's eight fell to that catch-all. On *PR already existed* the run pushed and a pull request is open, and the catch-all never named it — strictly weaker than the neighbouring *Committed, pushed, PR opened* row, on the rule's own standard that the clause "must name it truthfully". On *No remote* the run committed on a local branch and nothing was pushed, and the catch-all named neither that branch nor the fact that nothing in that repository will ever push it — while the paragraph under the table reasoned that a row naming no branch is one where `handoff-to-main` committed nothing, which is exactly why *No remote*, which does commit, had to name one. A live run met the second case in both of its commands and printed the weaker clause each time. **Population: every next-step offer printed on a run whose §4.1 line was *PR already existed* or *No remote* — on 1.7.0 and before.** Both rows are now in the table; the paragraph below it states the property — a row names a branch where that outcome committed one — and sends the reader to count them off the table rather than off the sentence, which is what had gone stale. `/prd-proposal` carried its own copy of the old count and now states the same property.

### Fixed — a producer that copied a §4.3 array never reached the push-target probe that section requires first

`phase-handoff.md` §4.3 fixes four consent arrays and then, **after** all four, requires §2.1's push-target probe to run before whichever one is presented, plus a notice line where that probe finds no `origin`. A reader who goes to §4.3, finds their artifact's class, copies its array and stops never reaches that paragraph — and measured across the tree, of the fifteen commands that quote one of those arrays, fourteen name no probe and no notice. `product-workflows`' `/brd-interview` is the observed case, found by a live run of it: it offered a `(Recommended)` push-and-open-PR on a specs repository with no remote, silently, which is the state §2.1 records that probe as having been added for. **Population: every producer that reaches §4.3 by its array on a specs repository with no `origin` remote.** §4.3 now names both steps immediately above the first array, where a reader selecting one meets them; the steps themselves are unmoved, and nothing is restated — the paragraph points at them rather than copying them, so §5 rule 4 is intact.

### Changed — two references name the agent that now reads the family's pictures, and the transcriptions `/brd-intake` records from it

`grounding-format.md` §6.1 and §6.2 named `idea-reader` as the agent reading the images an idea source links. `product-workflows` 3.7.0 moved that job to its new `figure-reader`, which also transcribes the images a customer's BRD links — directly or through a page it links — for `/brd-intake`, and both sections now name it: §6.1's paragraph on reading a picture says which of the family's picture readers each claim is true of, and that `brd/brd-figures.md` — the file `/brd-intake` writes from those transcriptions — is not a frame-set index and sits in no `design/` directory, so the index obligation does not reach it; §6.1's account of when an idea-route PRD folder holds a frame set now says an image the run's link walk took and `figure-reader` read; and §6.2's writer table says `/idea`'s per-image description is `figure-reader`'s `depicts` sentence, with the sentence under it saying that an image `/idea` never had transcribed is not copied, so it reaches step 4 only as a copy an earlier run or a person left in the set without a row — it had said such an image lands on step 4 outright, which a file never copied cannot. `phase-handoff.md` §4.0's register classifies the two artifacts `/brd-intake` gains, `brd/source-external/*` and `brd/brd-figures.md`, as **advisory** beside `brd/source/*`, naming `/brd-package`, a later `/brd-intake` run and `/brd-reconcile` as their readers, `/brd-reconcile` correcting the figures file on a customer's section-12 row and reading it and every markdown file under `brd/source-external/` in its stale-reference sweep, never editing a captured file — an artifact absent from that table is unclassified, and `/brd-intake`'s handoff declares both. §3.4's `/brd-package` row now says a folder with no `decisions.md` is one no interview has written a register for, where it said no interview had run: `product-workflows` 3.7.0's `/brd-interview` writes the register on every run that records a round, and before it a round of `[C]` questions alone left none. Its round-records row, and §4.0's register row for `interview/round-<N>.md`, now name the rounds `/brd-package` gates as those the register's records **or the held `[C]` entries** carry — they named the register alone, which a round of held `[C]` questions puts nothing in. No behaviour of any `workflows-core` command or agent changes.

### Fixed — a frame-set index named the linking file by a path on the operator's machine

`grounding-format.md` §6.2 defined a row's `Linked from` as "the original path of the file that carried that link", **never repointed at the copy**, while its own example row showed a relative path; `product-workflows`' `/idea`, which writes the column, took the absolute path of that file on the operator's machine, so every row it wrote named a file that resolves for nobody else reading the record. §6.2 now names the linking file by a path relative to the folder holding `design/` — the copy the writer vendored of it, or the file itself where it already sat in that folder — never by a path on the operator's machine, and `—` where no copy of it is in the folder; the example row reads `attachments/dark-mode.md`, and reconciliation step 3 calls the description, not the `Linked from` value, *transcribed verbatim*. **Population: every `/idea` run that vendors an image, from `product-workflows` 3.7.0, whose `idea-format.md` writes the new form**; a row an earlier run wrote keeps its absolute path, because §6.2 step 2 preserves every described row whose frame is still there verbatim, and §6.2 now says so. `/frames` holds no link for any frame: every row it appends reads `—`, a placeholder row it fills keeps its `—`, and every described row whose frame is still in the set is preserved as it stands — which §6.2 step 3 and `/frames` Phase 2 now say where a row is appended or filled — so no behaviour of any `workflows-core` command or agent changes.

### Fixed — `/frames` justified its cap against an `/idea` cap that is gone

`/frames` Phase 2 step 2 explained its 40-frame cap as *"not `/idea`'s 6"* — a cap bounding an incidental read, *"six mockups is generous for that"* — and its documentation page said `/idea` *"caps at six"*. `product-workflows` 3.7.0 removed that cap: `/idea` now asks its operator before reading past six images and reads what they allow, ten to a `figure-reader` dispatch. Both now give the reason that still holds: `/idea` asks and reads in batches of ten, while `/frames` asks nothing before it reads and describes a whole set in one `frame-describer` dispatch, so its cap is what bounds that context. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of either** — an operator or an agent weighing the cap against a comparison that no longer exists.

### Fixed — `/frames`' cost attribution was described by folder, where it is decided by the kind a folder asserts

`cost-emission.md` §7 decides `/frames`' `phase` by the resolved folder's own `kind`, and its two bullets, the command page and `docs/roles-and-phases.md` described the outcome by folder — a BRD folder's frame set is `brd-to-prd`, a PRD or Epic folder's `prd-creation`. A BRD-route slice is a `PRD-` folder whose `brd-link.md` asserts `kind: brd`, so its frame set is `brd-to-prd`, the opposite of what *"a PRD folder's"* says, while an Epic folder under a slice asserts `epic` and is `prd-creation`, which nothing said. Each now names the kind: a folder asserting `brd`, a container or a slice, is `brd-to-prd`; one asserting `prd` or `epic` is `prd-creation`, an Epic folder under a slice included, and `/frames` Phase 4's emission step says both. `dev-workflows`' session-cost page carried the same sentence and is corrected with it. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of the descriptions.**

### Fixed — the roles page said what a phase `/frames` charges means by a folder's progress, which the folder need not have

`docs/roles-and-phases.md`'s *prd-creation* said being in the phase means the PRD does not yet have a merged specification or design. `/frames` lands there on any folder asserting `kind: prd` or `kind: epic` (`cost-emission.md` §7), and an Epic folder it indexes may hold a merged specification or design already. The page now says what the phase means on every path that reaches it: the PM's product-definition spend on a PRD or one of its Epics — refining an idea, authoring the PRD, or indexing the frame sets that are the folder's own design record — and nothing about how far the folder has come. Its *brd-to-prd* said the phase means a BRD's inventory is being extracted, grounded, split, decided, packaged or reconciled, while `/frames` lands there on a slice that may already hold its PRD; it now names indexing a frame set in one of the BRD's folders too, and says the same of progress. `product-workflows`' page carried both sentences and is corrected in its 3.7.0. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of the page.**

### Fixed — `next-phase-offer` routed a PRD-level `/design` to `/implement` by an Epic it does not have

The *Dev* routing line read `/dev-workflows:design <EPIC>` → optionally `/dev-workflows:ready <EPIC>` → `/dev-workflows:implement <EPIC>`, while a broad PRD-level design — `/design` addressed to a `PRD-` folder holding a flat `specification.md` — has no Epic, so a command following the line offered a null key. It now says each takes `<PRD>` in place of `<EPIC>` after a PRD-level design, the folder the design was written into; `dev-workflows` 4.1.2's `/design` makes the same offer. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of the routing line.**

### Fixed — `ard-resolution` said `/epics` resolves its ARD before any Epic exists

The *Consumers* note gave `/epics`' `epic: null` the reason *"Epics do not exist yet"*, which a re-refine run — an `EPIC-` address, whose Epic exists — contradicts. It now says `epic: null` holds on a re-refine run as on a draft one, and that `prd` is the PRD folder's key either way, the key `product-workflows` 3.7.0's `/epics` now passes in place of the run's own. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of the note.**

### Fixed — `prd-format` listed a provenance nothing writes

`prd-format.md`'s propagated `sources` listed `provenance: rfe` with an `RFE key` ref. A PRD's `sources` are carried from its `idea.md`, and no command has written `rfe` into one since `/idea` stopped typing tracker exports; `product-workflows` 3.7.0 drops it from `idea-format.md` too. The list now carries the four values a run writes, and the ref is described as `idea.md` recorded it — absent on a prompt entry. The template also shows `vendored`, which `idea.md` records where `/idea` copied its source and `/create-prd` propagates with the rest of the entry where that `idea.md` is the one in the PRD's own folder — the path is relative to it — and drops otherwise. A value already in a PRD stays readable, since nothing decides anything on it. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of the PRD frontmatter template.**

### Fixed — `phase-handoff` printed its no-remote notice from a probe that ran after the choice it was printed above

§4.3 said to print *"This specs repo has no `origin` remote …"* above the consent array "where §2.1 set `remote: none`" — but §2.1 belongs to `handoff-to-main`, which §2 says runs **only** once the operator has picked option 1, so the probe that decides the notice could never have run before the array it is printed above, and no producer said to run it early. Three `/idea` verification runs on a specs repo with no remote each had to notice the gap and run the probe on their own initiative. §4.3 now opens its remote paragraph with the order — run §2.1's read-only probe before presenting the array, and print nothing where `$SPECS_PATH` is no git repository at all, which §2.1's gate reports if option 1 is taken — and §2's opening and §2.1's probe paragraph both say the probe is the one step of that entry point that runs ahead of the choice, its `remote` value carried into §2.5 and §2.6 rather than probed twice. Every producer inherits it by citation: none restates the notice or the probe. **Population: every run of the seventeen `handoff-to-main` producers that reaches §4.3 against a specs repo with no `origin` remote** — `/workflows-core:frames` among them; on a repo that has one, nothing changes.

### Fixed — a declined handoff's outcome line named one file, and opened its second sentence in lower case

§4.1's *Declined by the user* line had room for one `<artifact>`, while `/idea` declines `idea.md` plus every vendored copy (twenty-five paths in one verification run) and `/brd-intake` declines the document, every file it captured and the records it wrote beside them; every run improvised a name for the set. The line now reads *"this run's deliverable (`<artifacts>`) is written but not on `<default>`"*, `<artifacts>` being every declared `deliverable_paths` entry where there are three or fewer and `<n> files under <dir>` beyond three, `<dir>` the nearest directory holding every declared path — the run's feature folder, or one above it where the set spans folders, as `/brd-reconcile`'s second handoff can. A decline stages nothing, so the declaration is the only list there is. Its `<next-phase-clause>` followed a full stop, and all four values began in lower case; each is now written capitalised, and the singular subject lets each keep its *it*. **Population: every declined handoff, by every one of the seventeen producers.**

### Fixed — the **gated — falling back** array promised that the next phase reads an un-landed file, and for `idea.md` it does so only out of contract

§4.3's falling-back array, and §4.1's clause it must agree with, told the operator the next phase *"reports the artifact as un-landed and proceeds from the resolved folder"*. The array exists for `/idea`'s refined brief, and `/create-prd <KEY>` on that brief's row F names the file and does not read it in contract: it goes on down its idea ladder — back to the brief only through its same-session rung or a path the operator types, otherwise to a PRD grilled from scratch. The other two artifacts in that class split: an ARD on no ref is `status: none`, so its consumers read no ARD and print nothing, and `/implement` reads an in-scope `design.md` as it did before the gate while `/ready` records it missing. The option now reads *"the next phase does not stop on this, but until this is on main it might not read your copy"* and the clause *"The next phase does not stop on that: it falls back to what it does when the deliverable is missing, and that might not read your copy"*, with §4.1's bullet naming what each consumer does with the copy; the two sentences that described a wrong pick of it say *"describes a fallback by a phase that was never waiting on the file"* in place of the retired wording. §3.4's `/create-prd` row now says an `idea.md` in the folder but on no ref is named and not read in contract — `/create-prd` reaches it only through its same-session rung or a path the operator types, and `/create-prd <KEY> @<path>` reads it directly. `ard-resolution.md`'s *Output* said `unmerged` whenever an ARD resolves and is not on the default branch, which on a literal reading took in row F — the state a declined `/create-ard` leaves — and would have stopped every consumer but `/ready` naming a branch that does not exist; it now names rows D and E for `unmerged` and row F for `none`, as §3.4 already recorded. `next-phase-offer.md`'s routing graph gave `/idea`'s draft branch as `/idea @<path> --deep` and a bare `/create-prd <KEY>` — neither what `/idea` offers, and the second a form that names the draft without reading it — and now names `/idea <KEY> <the same source>` and `/create-prd <KEY> @<path to idea.md>`, the refined branch gaining the same `@<path>` route where the handoff was declined or its gate failed. **Population: every run that presents the array — `/idea` on `status: refined`, `/create-ard` whose set is the ARD alone, and `/implement` Phase 4.5 whose set is an annotated `design.md` alone — for the option, and every one of those whose handoff was declined for the clause**; the array's quoted copies in `dev-workflows`' `/implement` and `product-workflows`' `/create-ard` move with it, recorded in those plugins' changelogs.

### Fixed — a root BRD folder was unresolvable on a strict reading of `addressing` §4, and "the first artifact" named no order

§4 read a folder's `kind` and `key` off "the first artifact in the folder carrying both fields" and passed every reserved subdirectory over, while a root BRD's only artifact naming a folder kind is `brd/brd-inventory.md`, which `/brd-intake` writes inside the reserved `brd/` — no command writes one at a root's top level. Nothing fixed which artifact was first either, so a slice holding both `brd-link.md` (`kind: brd`) and `prd.md` (`kind: prd`) could resolve either way, and an idea-route PRD folder holding `ard.md` could resolve as kind `ard`, a value the resolution record has no place for. §4 now names the carrier in order: the folder's top-level files in byte-wise order of name, the first whose frontmatter holds `key:` and a `kind:` naming a folder kind (`brd`, `prd` or `epic`); else `brd/brd-inventory.md`, the one file below the top level ever read for this, read for the folder `brd/` sits in and never for `brd/` itself. **The retired half is itemised, since a rewrite that narrows a rule is a deletion**: a resolver may no longer read the pair "off whichever artifact it had to open anyway", because two resolvers could open different artifacts in one slice and read different kinds; byte-wise order puts `brd-link.md` first, which is the reading the family's container refusals already gave as their reason for testing a directory prefix — a slice asserts `brd`. §2's reserved-subdirectory paragraph now says the subdirectories' *names* carry no key. `prd-format.md` said an unset `key` left a slice "resolving as a BRD rather than a PRD" — implying that setting it makes the slice resolve as a PRD. The defined order puts a slice's `brd-link.md` before its `prd.md` whether or not `prd.md` carries a key, so a slice resolves as `brd` either way, and that contrast is what was false; it now names the defect that was real, the empty commit-scan key, and says the folder still resolved. `dev-workflows`' `design-format.md` and `product-workflows`' `specification-format.md` each said their file's `kind:` and `key:` are *"how the folder asserts its own identity"*, which §4 now passes over for `kind: design` and `kind: specification`; each says instead that `kind:` names the document, recorded in those plugins' changelogs, as are `/create-prd`'s copy of the `prd-format` sentence and `brd-format.md`'s inventory header. **Population: every `resolve-address` of a root BRD whose top level carries no artifact naming a folder kind, as every root `/brd-intake` writes does, and every resolution of a folder whose top level holds two** — on the latter the order now fixes the answer every documented reading already assumed.

§4's *Why step 2 exists* said a root BRD's **only keyed artifact** is the inventory inside `brd/`. `product-workflows` 3.7.0 fixes the layout of the coverage ledger `/brd-intake` writes at the folder's top level, and gives it frontmatter — `key:` beside a `kind:` naming the document, `coverage-ledger` — so the ledger is keyed too. Step 1 passes it over, as it passes over every document kind, and the sentence now says the inventory is the only artifact naming a **folder** kind, and a root's only carrier: it had said so only *until a later command writes one at the top level*, and no command does. **No resolution changes. Population: every reader of that sentence.**

The documentation page's reference list said `prd-format.md` carries *"the `kind:`/`key:` frontmatter every artifact carries so its folder asserts its own identity"* — the reading §4 retired, since a document kind is passed over. It now says `key:` records the folder's key, `kind: prd` names the document, and §4 reads the folder's identity off the PRD only where no top-level file before it in byte-wise order names a folder kind — a slice's `brd-link.md` and an `idea.md` both do. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of that page.**

### Fixed — no reference said what places a folder at a level, and the callers' copies disagreed

`addressing.md` §4 said a folder's `kind:` and `key:` are read off its carrier and never parsed out of its name, and nothing in the file said what decides the level a run works at — which on a BRD-route slice is not the kind its carrier asserts, `brd`. Each caller stated its own rule, and the copies disagreed: `dev-workflows`' `/ready` placed a folder with no prefix by positive evidence, `product-workflows`' `/create-ard` by its prefix alone with no case for a folder that has none, and `/specify`, `/design` and this plugin's `next-phase-offer.md` said the kind decides. **§4.1 now owns the rule.** The level is the folder's prefix — its name beginning `<KIND>-<key>-`, the key being the one §4 read, so a legacy folder whose key begins with a kind token is not prefixed — and a folder with none, one §5's fallback resolved or an unprefixed folder an `@<path>` names, is placed by positive evidence in a fixed order: a BRD container first, by `product-workflows:coverage-ledger-format` §5.1's test; then a resolved `kind: epic`, Epic-level; then a resolved `kind: prd` or a `brd-link.md` naming a `parent:`, PRD-level. A folder none of them places is never guessed at, and a refusal naming the slices under a container finds them by `/brd-split`'s positive test, never by a name match. `next-phase-offer.md` rule 7 now says the prefix sets the altitude and cites §4.1, and `phase-handoff.md` §3.4's build-ladder paragraph says the positive-evidence test also takes an unprefixed folder an `@<path>` names. **No behaviour of any `workflows-core` command or agent changes; the callers' behaviour is recorded in `product-workflows` 3.7.0 and `dev-workflows` 4.1.2. Population: every reader of the rule.**

### Fixed — `specs-repo-git` quoted `phase-handoff` §2.1 in words it never held

§3.2's paragraph on the two default-ref cases said §2.1's push-target probe is explicitly *"a capability probe, never a gate failure"*. `phase-handoff.md` §2.1 never held that phrase, and a bare §2.1 in `specs-repo-git.md` names that file's own §2.1, the bounded write authority. It now names `phase-handoff.md` §2.1 and quotes its heading — *"run before the choice, and never gated on"* — saying in its own words that a repository with no `origin` is not a gate failure there. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader following the quotation.**

### Fixed — `grounding-format` §6.2's index template carried its prose paragraph hard-wrapped

The paragraph under the template's heading was wrapped at this reference's width, so a writer copying the template verbatim wrote hard-wrapped prose into every frame-set index, against `prose-formatting.md`. It is one line now, and a sentence under the template says a writer copies it as one line. **Population: every frame-set index `/workflows-core:frames` and `product-workflows`' `/idea` write from here on**; an index already written keeps its wrapped paragraph until its next rebuild.

### Fixed — `phase-handoff` §3.4 made `interview/customer-questions.md` a `/brd-package` gate target

The round-records row named `decisions.md` and `interview/customer-questions.md` in its **Input** column as the sources of the round set, and `scripts/check-docs.sh` check 11 reads that column's backticked filenames as each command's `require-on-main` targets — so the build took the questions file for a `/brd-package` gate target while §4.0 classes it advisory, which it is: `/brd-package` reads it for its rounds and gates nothing on it. The column now names the round record alone, and the sources, with the sentence saying the questions file is read and not gated, sit in the behaviour column. Check 11's derived targets for `/brd-package` fall from three to two, `round-<N>.md` and `decisions.md` (the latter from the register's own row). **No run's behaviour changed. Population: check 11, and any reader of the row.**

### Fixed — `grounding-format` §2.1's reading of an empty value was stated for every record

§2.1 said an empty value asserts that a field applies and its value is unknown, with no scope, while `product-workflows`' decision register writes `evidence: []` as a known empty list — a decision resting on no finding — and says it is not the case §2.1 omits a field for. Read as written, the two disagreed about one spelling. §2.1 now fixes its reading for finding records only, and says a register borrowing a field name may give its own empty value a meaning of its own. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader weighing an empty field outside a finding record.**

### Changed — `escalation-rules` names the confirmation picker's form for an answer matching no question

`product-workflows` 3.7.0's `/brd-reconcile` never freezes an answer matching no question the package put, and puts such a candidate to the operator on a two-option form of its confirmation picker — record it for a human, or reject it. The closed-vocabulary table's first row now names that form beside the four-option one, so a free-text answer on it is normalised into those two, re-points the candidate onto a question the package put where it names one — which re-asks it on the four-option form, the route for a reader that mis-matched it — or is re-asked, and is never frozen on that form. That change added no array to the table — it named a second form of a row already there. **Population: every `/brd-reconcile` run whose review answers something the package did not ask.**

### Changed — `phase-handoff` §4.1 counts its outcome line per handoff offered, and says which runs print none

§4.1 opened *"Exactly one, prefixed `Phase handoff:`"* and left two things to the reader, both of which were read wrong in shipped text. **It said nothing about a run that never reaches the offer**, so `/workflows-core:frames` asked its Final Report for the line *"when the handoff ran, or the fact that it was declined"* — prose standing in for §4.1's own *Declined by the user* row — while a correction in the other direction was about to demand the line *on every run*, which a producer that never offers a handoff cannot print without asserting a decline nobody was asked about. **And its count was per run**, which `product-workflows` 3.7.0's `/brd-reconcile` falsified by design: it hands off the canonicalised customer review, then its own reconciliation record on the branch the first created (§2.2 rule 3) — two deliverables, two consent choices, two outcomes that can differ — and emits a labelled line for each. §4.1 now counts **per handoff the run offers**: the rows are the taken outcomes; a decline is inside that scope, the *Declined by the user* row emitted by the producer on either declining option (§4.3 cited as the authority rather than copied); a handoff never offered prints no line and deliberately has no row, the three live cases being `/frames` with no index written, `/idea` on `status: draft`, and `/implement`'s Phase 4.5 no-op; and where a run offers more than one, each line names which handoff it reports. Re-derived rather than assumed: `/brd-reconcile` is the only command in the family executing `handoff-to-main` twice, so for the other sixteen producers the per-offer and per-run counts are the same number, which is why the rule read "one per run" until a run emitted two. `next-phase-offer.md` resolved `<merge-clause>` from *"the"* line §4.1 emitted, which has no referent on a two-handoff run — a live run had to choose and took its own; it now resolves from the line reporting the handoff of the artifact the offered command's gate targets, which for `/brd-reconcile` is the reconciliation record's, since its offers name `/product-workflows:brd-package` and `/product-workflows:prd-ground`. `/frames`' Final Report asks for the line itself on both outcomes its offer reaches and says a run that wrote no index prints neither. **Population: every reader of §4.1 and of `next-phase-offer`'s clause table — the seventeen producers that call `handoff-to-main` — plus every `/frames` run that wrote no index, every `/idea` run on `status: draft`, every `/implement` run whose Phase 4.5 is a no-op, and every `/brd-reconcile` run, whose two lines had no stated resolution before this.**

### Changed — `escalation-rules`' closed-vocabulary table gains `/brd-package`'s customer-content ruling, and the ordinals move with it

`product-workflows` 3.7.0's `/brd-package` asks the operator once per pass that reports a hit inside the customer's own words or naming — ship the package, or hold it — and `bundle-packaging.md` §6.3's *The operator's ruling* already required that a free-text answer be read onto one of the two or the question asked again. That is exactly the property the *Closed-vocabulary pickers must normalise the free-text answer* section exists to record, and the table did not carry it. It is now row 7, beside the other two `/brd-package` rows, so the `engagement_model` picker becomes row 8, and every ordinal and count in the prose below the table moves with it — the sentences naming the configuration-valued row and its *second route to the same field*, and each sentence that states how many arrays the table holds. **Row 6 is no longer alone in being spent inside its own run** — that claim now names rows 6 and 7 and gives each its reason: row 6's tier is a fact about one dated bundle, and row 7's ruling binds the passes of the run that took it and reaches that run's final report as an outcome line, nothing reading it back. The list stays closed, and this marks where a free-text answer needs normalising, never where an array may be shortened. **No run's behaviour changed — `/brd-package` already normalised that answer, and this records where. Population: every reader of the table, and every `/brd-package` run whose pass reports a customer-content hit.**

### Fixed — `phase-handoff` §4.0 gave the link log a reader through a read that now falls back to it

§4.0's advisory row said `/brd-split` Phase 3 step 4 reads `brd/brd-link-log.md`, its slice-inventory header naming the parent's source document by the basename on the log's opening line. `product-workflows` 3.7.0 takes that name from the inventory's `document:` — the document the rows were last reconciled against, which the opening line misnames after a rename run that stopped before its Phase 3 — so `/brd-split`, and `/brd-package` for the bundled document's name, read the opening line only over an inventory carrying no `document:`, one written before 3.7.0 that no 3.7.0 intake has run over, and a later `/brd-intake` reads it there to write that inventory its `document:`. The row now gives that as the reason, beside the read that holds on every inventory: `/brd-package`'s *Assemble the bundle* phase finding, through the log's *Captured links that do not resolve as written* table, each image a captured file reaches through a `[[wikilink]]` or from outside the document's own directory. The class is unchanged — advisory — since none of those reads is a gate. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of the row.**

### Changed — `implementation-format` says where the record lives, and whose a block an earlier run left in the PRD folder is

§1 fixed the record's shape and said nothing of its folder, which `/implement` took to be the one its address resolved — the PRD folder even where it chose an Epic there, through its picker or as the PRD's only Epic, while the picker's ● marker, `/ready`'s per-Epic inventory and an Epic-level `/document` or `/release-notes` read the Epic's own folder. `dev-workflows` 4.1.2 writes into the folder of the unit implemented, and §1 now says so: an Epic's folder, however the Epic was chosen, or the PRD folder for a broad PRD-level slice, a file the run creates opening with that folder's key and slug. A block names no unit, so the folder is what attributes one, and a block an earlier run left in the PRD folder for an Epic counts as the PRD folder's — a PRD-level reader reads it, an Epic-level reader does not, no run moves it, and the picker's ● marker, which reads the Epic's own folder, does not count it. §1 now states what that costs until the block is moved, **for every Epic implemented under a PRD address before `dev-workflows` 4.1.2**: the Epic reads as not implemented to everything Epic-level — `/implement`'s picker marks it ◐ or ○, a ◐ row taking the default cursor, `/ready` derives its phase from a folder holding no record, an Epic-level `/document` or `/release-notes` reads none of its refs — and, **for every PRD folder holding both a flat `specification.md` and such a block, from `dev-workflows` 4.1.2**, a PRD-level `/ready` counts the block as the broad slice's own record, which moves the slice to *In Progress* only where the PRD folder also holds a `design.md`, Phase 3(0) taking the furthest rung whose expected artifacts all exist. And it names the way out, which is the operator's: **move** the block by hand — cut it from the PRD folder's `implementation.md` and append it to the Epic folder's, under the heading a run would write where the file is new — since append-only binds the commands, not the operator; a copy left behind is harmless to the diff readers, which count a ref two records name once (§4), but `/ready` would still count it toward the slice. §1 also defines a record's presence once: a file holds a record only where it holds at least one block, a file holding only its heading — what a move can leave in the PRD folder — recording nothing; `/implement`'s ● marker, `epic-picker.md`'s ● bullet and `/ready`'s inventory cite it. The opening paragraph, which named `/document` and `/release-notes` as the readers, now also names the four that read the file for whether it holds a block or for the repositories its entries name — `epic-picker.md`'s marker, `/ready`, `/specify` and `/epics` — the census that says who depends on where it lives. **Population: every reader of §1, and every keyed `/implement` run given a PRD address that chose an Epic, through `dev-workflows` 4.1.2.**

### Fixed — `implementation-format` keyed a commit to the folder the address resolved, and a PRD-level scan grepped that one key

§3 said the commit subject ends `[<key>]`, with a `Work-Item:` trailer *"when the resolved folder carries one"*, and §4 said the scan's keys *"come from the resolved folder's own `key:`"*. A run that implements an Epic `/implement` chose under a PRD address resolved the PRD folder, so the convention keyed its commit to the PRD while §1 puts its record in the Epic's folder; and a PRD-level read, which takes the PRD folder's record and every Epic's under it, grepped the PRD's key alone, as an unanchored substring. That substring already reached the Epic keys `/epics` mints by extending the PRD's (`<PRD-KEY>-NN`); what it could not see was an Epic's `workitem_key`, or an Epic key that does not extend its PRD's. And it over-matched: a PRD key `ACME-7` also found `[ACME-77]` and `[ACME-70-01]`, a `workitem_key` `PROJ-12` found `PROJ-123`, and each such commit reached `diff-summarizer` as the scope's unrecorded work. §3 now says the key is the unit's — the Epic's wherever the run implemented an Epic, even under a PRD address, and otherwise the folder the address resolved — and that the trailer and the branch carry that unit's too, which `dev-workflows` 4.1.2's `/implement` writes. §4 now matches each token only as a whole key — `(^|[^A-Za-z0-9_-])<token>([^A-Za-z0-9_-]|$)`, the token's ERE metacharacters escaped, one `--grep` per token with `--extended-regexp` — and says why its boundary is stricter than `specs-repo-git.md` §3.5's `branch-key`, where a slug follows a key — and what that costs. The one thing that legitimately continues a key in a commit message is a branch name inside it, `Merge branch 'feat/ACME-7-order-intake'` as `branch-naming.md` §1.4 builds it, and admitting `-` or `_` to reach that would admit `[ACME-70-01]` again, so a branch whose own subjects carry no bracketed key is no longer found through its merge commit; §3's *"second recovery path"* for the branch name, and `branch-naming.md` §1.4's, now name who recovers through it — a person reading the log or the branch list, never the scan. And it takes the scan's keys from the records the read takes: an Epic-level read greps that Epic's key and its `workitem_key`, and a PRD-level read the PRD folder's and every `EPIC-` folder's, each with its `workitem_key`, every key read off a folder the run resolved or listed — the per-Epic greps being what find an Epic's commits, now that the PRD's key, matched whole, no longer reaches them. §4's *Merged and deduped by SHA* now says a ref two records name — the same repository and commit, as where an operator copied a block rather than moving it (§1) — is counted once by a read that takes both, which `docs-workflows` 1.2.2's `/document` and `/release-notes` cite. **Population: every PRD-level `/document` scan and every PRD-level `/release-notes` scan with diff grounding on, every `/implement` commit for an Epic chosen under a PRD address, and every reader of §3 and §4; and, for the over-match, every keyed `/document` scan and every `/release-notes` scan with diff grounding on in a repository holding a commit whose message names a longer token beginning with a key or `workitem_key` the run greps for — before this release too; and, for the loss the whole-key match introduces, every one of those scans over a repository where a key's work is carried by a branch name alone, from this release.**

### Fixed — `implementation-format` §4's release boundary bound the record and not the scan, and bounded both by a date

§4 gave `/release-notes` the blocks no earlier note covers, and said nothing of the commit scan beside them: every commit a covered block records came back from the scan as unrecorded work, since the blocks it is merged against were not read, and every hand-made commit an earlier note had described came back on every later run — against the bullet's own *"a second release must not re-describe the first one's work"*. And the boundary it gave the blocks was a **date**, which cannot say whether a note saw a commit: a branch committed on 09-01, described by no note written on 09-10, and merged onto the scanned history on 09-20 is dated inside the span that note covers, so it was dropped and no note ever described it; a block that reached the specs checkout after such a note was skipped the same way. §4 now says the boundary binds both sources and is **the set of commits an earlier note read**, never a date — `docs-workflows` 1.2.2's scope comment records that set per repository, as 12-character SHAs, and `release-note-types.md` §1 fixes its form. A block is skipped only where every commit it records is in the read set of an earlier note covering its record, the record's own notes and the PRD's; the scan drops every commit whose SHA a block in the records the read takes names, covered or not, and every commit in such a read set — the PRD folder's record for the PRD's key or `workitem_key`, an Epic's for that Epic's — and nothing else save what the one fallback below drops, so a commit is skipped only where an earlier note covering it read it, or where that fallback's date rule drops it — by its own date, or by the heading date of a block recording it, since a block the date rule skipped still takes its commits out of the scan — and the run says so. The one fallback is an earlier note that records no read set: it still bounds by date, now the committer date as `git log --date=short-local --format=%cd` prints it, in the scanning machine's own zone rather than each committer's, where `--date=short` moved a late-evening commit onto the wrong day; §4 states what that fallback loses and has the run list every block it dropped and every commit it dropped by the commit's own date, beside the ones it used, a commit dropped with a block recording it being accounted for by that block's listing. `/document` takes no note boundary, and §4 now says so: it reads every block in its scope, so merging by SHA already keeps each recorded commit out of the unrecorded work. The documentation page's reference list now says the scan matches each key whole and the boundary covers both sources. **Population: every `/release-notes` run with diff grounding on, over a scope an earlier note covers — before this release too. The fallback's own population: every such run over a `release-notes.md` an earlier release wrote, which is every one that exists today, and no note written from this release on.**

### Fixed — `implementation-format` §4 gave `/release-notes` one boundary across a PRD's Epics, and `/document` a scope it holds only at PRD level

§4 said `/release-notes` reads *"only blocks appended since its last section was written"*, the file's last-written date the boundary. `release-notes.md` is one per PRD, and `docs-workflows` 1.2.2 lets an Epic address draft a note, so a note for one Epic moved every other Epic's boundary. §4 now takes the boundary per record: a note for the PRD covers every record and a note for an Epic that Epic's alone, each note's scope read off the scope comment `docs-workflows`' `release-note-types.md` §1 now defines, and what a note covers within its scope is the set of commits it read (the entry above). And §4 said `/document` *"reads every block under the PRD"*, which an Epic-level run, reading its Epic's record alone, does not; it now reads every block under its scope. **Population: every `/release-notes` run with diff grounding on over a PRD carrying a note drafted at another scope, and every reader of §4.**

### Fixed — `docs-grounder`'s git-log backstop matched a key as a substring

Path B's backstop — the one reached when `qmd` is absent, off, or missed — ran `git log --all -E --grep="<KEY>"` over `$DOCS_PATH` with no boundary on the token, so a key `ACME-7` also matched a docs commit naming `ACME-77` or `ACME-70-01`, and the pages those commits touched were unioned into the shortlist as grounding for another feature. It now runs the whole-key form `implementation-format.md` §4 defines and cites it — `(^|[^A-Za-z0-9_-])<KEY>([^A-Za-z0-9_-]|$)`, the key's ERE metacharacters escaped — which is the same correction `docs-workflows` 1.2.2 made to `diff-summarizer`'s key-commit fallback. **Population: every Path-B grounding run carrying a key, over a docs history naming a longer key that begins with it — before this release too. Docs grounding is advisory and gates nothing (`docs-grounding.md`), so what an over-match cost was a shortlist ranked on the wrong pages, never a stop.**

### Changed — `implementation-format` §4 recovers the whole-key scan's one blind spot as a report, where it finds nothing at all

§4's whole-key match, new in this release, cannot see a key that a commit message carries only inside a branch name — `Merge branch 'feat/ACME-7-order-intake'` — because admitting the `-` that follows it would admit `[ACME-70-01]` with it. Matching a branch *form* instead was refused on three grounds §4 now states: a repository's own documented convention wins over `branch-naming.md` §1.4's shape (§1.1 there), so there is no form to derive; the loss lives mostly in branches people named by hand, which no derived form matches; and where one would match, those commits already carry their key and the scan already has them. What §4 adds instead is a **report-only** probe, run **only** over a repository the whole-key scan left at zero matches — the one state in which that loss is indistinguishable from a repository holding no work for this run's tokens: the same `git log` over that repository with its tokens bare — the set the scan used there, each still ERE-escaped — every match printed by its SHA, date and subject as *"may name this key inside a branch name — inspect by hand"*. Nothing is read — no commit reaches `diff-summarizer`, none enters a `/release-notes` run's read set, and none enters a drop set — so the probe owes no boundary rule and leaves a later run exactly as it found it. `docs-workflows` 1.2.2 wires it into `/document`'s and `/release-notes`' *Report the scan's own reach* step, which already had the zero-match case in view. **Population: every `/document` scan, and every `/release-notes` scan with diff grounding on, over a repository where the whole-key scan matches nothing — from this release, and reporting only.**

### Fixed — `epic-picker` counted `/specify`'s own option against the cap and not `/implement`'s, and said one Epic is always auto-selected

*The cap* says a command that adds its own option counts it against the four, and named `/specify` alone; `/implement` adds *"Implement one broad PRD-level slice instead"* too, and its array carried three Epic rows beside it and the remainder option. It now names both, and separates them, since `dev-workflows` 4.1.2 offers `/implement`'s slice option only where the PRD folder holds a flat `specification.md`: `/specify`'s array carries at most two Epic rows on every run, and `/implement`'s two where that file stands and three where it does not, the freed row going back to the Epics. The sentence saying which run overflows at four names both, and *What the picker does not do*'s "always an explicit option" is scoped to wherever the command offers it. The *Exactly one* branch said the command auto-proceeds; `dev-workflows` 4.1.2's `/implement` asks between the Epic and a broad PRD-level slice where the PRD folder also holds a flat `specification.md`, and the branch now names that exception. **Population: every reader of the two sections, and every `/implement` run given a PRD address of four or more Epics where the PRD folder holds a flat `specification.md`, or five or more where it does not, through `dev-workflows` 4.1.2** — the two thresholds *The cap*'s own *overflows at four* sentence gives, counted against `escalation-rules` §0's four-option ceiling: appending, four Epic rows plus that option is five, so four overflows; not appending, four fit and five do not.

### Fixed — `implementation-format` §4 stated no cost for its two boundaries' different granularities, and compared an abbreviated SHA against a full one

§4 bounds `/release-notes` twice over — a **block** is skipped only where every commit it records is in an earlier note's read set, while the scan drops **every commit** in such a read set — so a note that covered a block only partly leaves the block to be taken whole, its covered ref handed to `diff-summarizer` again and a change the earlier note already described summarised a second time, against the bullet's own *reads only what no earlier note covering it read*. Reachable because a read set holds each block's `commit:` **whose diff the run read**, and `docs-workflows:release-note-types` §1 says a commit the run could not resolve is not written into it: a block naming two repositories where one clone did not resolve leaves one covered commit and one uncovered. The block-granular test stands — a block is one `/implement` run's refs, and splitting it would hand the writer a run with a hole in it — and §4 now states what it costs and over what, and says `Blocks used:` names such a block as it names any other. **Population: every `/release-notes` run with diff grounding on over a note that covered a block only partly** — a multi-repository block plus one unresolvable repository or one failed dispatch. §4 also rested two rules on SHA identity while §1's template writes `commit:` abbreviated to seven characters, and said nowhere that the abbreviation must be resolved first; the project wrote that rule down only in `release-note-types.md` §1, scoped to the read-set comparison, in a file `/document` never loads — so a keyed `/document` run comparing `a3f91c2` against a 40-character SHA by equality dropped no recorded commit at all and reported each as unrecorded work on a run whose record is complete. §4's *Merged and deduped by SHA* now binds every comparison against a block's `commit:`: resolve it in its own repository with `git rev-parse` and compare the resolved values, `release-note-types.md` §1's 12-character read-set form cited as that comparison's own case. **Population: every keyed `/document` scan and every `/release-notes` scan with diff grounding on over a record holding a block, from this release's own drop and before it. No run's behaviour changes on either half: the first states a cost, the second a rule its consumers were already meant to follow.**

### Fixed — `phase-handoff` §4.1 said every other producer executes its handoff once, two paragraphs below three that may reach no offer

§4.1's two-handoff paragraph closed *"every other command in the family executes `handoff-to-main` once"*, an absolute its own preceding paragraph falsifies twice over: three producers may never reach an offer at all — `/frames` with no index written, `/idea` on `status: draft`, `/implement`'s Phase 4.5 no-op — and a declined offer prints its line while §2 never executes. The inference the sentence draws is unaffected, and live behaviour was never at risk, but two live contradictory statements is a defect (`instruction-file-maintenance` §4). It now reads **offers `handoff-to-main` at most once**, and says which two states the *at most* is there for. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of §4.1.** `product-workflows`' `/brd-reconcile` states the same absolute in its own words, in a file this plugin does not own.

### Fixed — `escalation-rules`' array table spelled out one picker's narrowed form and left two implicit

The eight-array table's first row already names the candidate-confirmation picker's two-option form, so a reader comparing rows takes an unqualified vocabulary cell to mean the array is always presented whole. Two rows had stopped meaning that on this branch. `product-workflows` 3.7.0's `/brd-reconcile` withholds `reopened` on an `[AS#n]` item — `decision-register-format` §7 makes it unreachable, and writing it would drop the assumption out of the open set the next package puts to the customer — and withholds `reverted` on an item whose prior position the record does not preserve, since §4 restores only what `argumentation` establishes and a field it cannot recover is never invented. Its `/brd-package` withholds `fixed` where the finding's named artifact is one that command may not change. All three vocabularies are unchanged, and every narrowed form still renders under §0's 2–4: what was missing was the table saying so where the first row already does. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of the table.**

### Fixed — `phase-handoff` §5 rule 1 restated §4.1's quantifier, and got it wrong in all three directions §4.1 settles

Rule 1 said a producer *"**executes** `handoff-to-main` (§2) … and emits the §4.1 line **exactly once**"*, ninety-six lines below the section it was quantifying. §4.1 settles three things against it: `/brd-reconcile` offers two handoffs and prints two lines; a producer that never reaches an offer prints none (`/frames` with no index, `/idea` on `status: draft`, `/implement`'s Phase 4.5 no-op); and §4.3's options 2 and 3 decline, on which §2 never executes while the line is still printed. Rule 1 now **offers**, and emits the outcome line **on §4.1's own count** — a citation rather than a copy, which is what rule 4 four lines below it requires of every rule in this section. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of §5.** The same absolute stood in `/brd-reconcile` and was removed in `product-workflows` 3.7.0.

### Fixed — `specs-repo-git` §4 said nothing about a run that refuses before it writes, and both readings were defensible

A live `/brd-intake` run hit a Phase 0 stop and found nothing anywhere saying whether the emitter tail — feedback, follow-ups, the cost entry, `resume.md`, `commit-artifacts` — still runs there.
